As true visibility falls from every scene to one in eight, the confidence stays nearly constant and uncorrelated with visibility, and the detector reports the target present in about nine of ten scenes, firing on same-category distractors: it signals that the category is present somewhere, not that the specific target is visible. The failure holds across three detectors (Grounding DINO, OWLv2, and Segment Anything Model 3), nine object categories, two simulators with different renderers and object sets, built and natural occlusion, and real video. Two consequences follow: a confidence-based metric understates the value of resolving occlusion by about ten times (8 against 88 points in our active-perception setting), and a confidence-based gate fires exactly when the object is hidden. No single-view signal we tried, including a realizable localization check, flags the occlusion, because the occluders sit where the target is. We connect the effect to detector miscalibration and object hallucination, release the controlled benchmark, and recommend target-grounded signals for gating and evaluation.

</details>


### [26] [DiffGI: Differentiable Geometry Images for High-Fidelity Thin-Shell 3D Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13365)
*Eungjune Shim,Hansol Lee,Eunjung Ju*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DiffGI replaces binary occupancy maps with a continuous 2D TSDF and differentiable Marching Squares for end-to-end 3D-to-2D mapping, achieving high-fidelity surface reconstruction and conditional 3D generation with ultra-compact latent spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D generative models rely on implicit volumetric representations that enforce watertight topology and struggle with thin-shell/non-manifold geometries (e.g., garments). Geometry image approaches use discrete binary occupancy maps causing staircase artifacts and non-differentiable reconstruction steps.

Method: (1) Replace binary occupancy maps with continuous 2D TSDF for subpixel boundary encoding. (2) Introduce differentiable Marching Squares via analytical linear interpolation. (3) Train DiffGI-VAE with geometry-aware normal rendering loss to compress surfaces into 32x32 latent space. (4) Use transformer-based latent diffusion model with flow-matching for conditional 3D generation.

Result: Superior reconstruction fidelity and boundary precision over prior geometry-image and voxel-based approaches on garment and object datasets, with significantly fewer computational resources.

Conclusion: DiffGI provides an end-to-end differentiable surface representation pipeline that eliminates staircase artifacts, enables gradient propagation to latent space, and achieves efficient high-quality conditional 3D generation.

Abstract: Existing 3D generative models predominantly rely on implicit volumetric representations, which enforce watertight topology and struggle to represent thin-shell and non-manifold geometries such as garments. Geometry image-based approaches offer a surface-centric alternative, but existing methods rely on discrete binary occupancy maps whose resolution-dependent boundary encoding causes staircase artifacts and information loss upon downsampling, while surface reconstruction remains a non-differentiable post-processing step disconnected from the learning pipeline. To address this, we propose Differentiable Geometry Image (DiffGI), an end-to-end 3D-to-2D mapping framework that seamlessly integrates surface representation and geometric optimization. DiffGI replaces binary maps with a continuous 2D Truncated Signed Distance Function (TSDF), which encodes boundary position at subpixel precision within a fixed grid resolution, eliminating resolution-dependent staircase artifacts even under aggressive downsampling. Building on this continuous field, we introduce a differentiable Marching Squares algorithm based on analytical linear interpolation, allowing gradients from 3D surface losses to propagate back to the 2D latent space. Leveraging this differentiable pipeline, we train a DiffGI-VAE augmented with a geometry-aware normal rendering loss to compress complex 3D surfaces into an ultra-compact 32X32 latent space, and instantiate a transformer-based latent diffusion model with a flow-matching objective on top of this space for conditional 3D generation. Extensive experiments on garment and object datasets demonstrate that our method achieves superior reconstruction fidelity and boundary precision compared to prior geometry-image and voxel-based approaches, while requiring significantly fewer computational resources.

</details>


### [27] [RoughNet: Mapping Arctic Sea Ice Roughness Using Diffusion-Based Super-Resolution of Satellite Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13371)
*Tessa Cannon,Michel Tsamados,Petru Manescu,Thomas Newman,Christian Haas,Veit Helm,Weibin Chen,Randall Scharien*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RoughNet uses a conditional diffusion framework to reconstruct 1 m resolution sea ice roughness from 10 m Sentinel-2 optical satellite imagery, achieving 9 cm RMSE out-of-domain and generalizing across Arctic regions without airborne surveys.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing landfast sea ice roughness estimation methods depend on costly airborne LiDAR surveys or sparse in-situ measurements, limiting spatial coverage and operational scalability for climate modeling and safe Arctic travel.

Method: A conditional diffusion model (RoughNet) maps 10 m Sentinel-2 multispectral images to locally normalized 1 m surface elevation residual fields, trained on airborne LiDAR data from two Arctic regions and evaluated on a third unseen region.

Result: The best model achieves 9 cm out-of-domain RMSE, preserves statistical and spectral roughness properties, generalizes across diverse ice conditions, and partially reproduces small-scale topographic structure.

Conclusion: Generative diffusion models can recover physically meaningful sea ice surface structure from optical imagery alone, offering a scalable, cost-effective alternative for high-resolution roughness mapping in data-sparse Arctic environments.

Abstract: Accurate estimation of landfast sea ice roughness is critical for climate modeling and safe Arctic over-ice travel, yet existing approaches rely on costly airborne surveys or sparse in-situ measurements, limiting spatial coverage and operational scalability. Here we show that high-resolution sea ice topography can be reconstructed directly from optical satellite imagery using a conditional diffusion framework. Our approach, RoughNet, learns to map 10 m Sentinel-2 multispectral images to locally normalized 1 m surface elevation residual fields, enabling fine-scale roughness characterization from widely available satellite data. Trained on airborne LiDAR data from two Arctic regions and evaluated on an unseen third Arctic region, the model generalizes across diverse ice conditions and partially reproduces small-scale topographic structure. The best-performing model achieves an out-of-domain root mean squared error of 9 cm while preserving the statistical and spectral properties of the underlying roughness field. These results demonstrate that generative diffusion models can recover physically meaningful surface structure from optical imagery alone, providing a scalable pathway for high-resolution sea ice mapping and roughness estimation in data-sparse environments.

</details>


### [28] [FM$^2$: Unified Federated Foundation Models for Heterogeneous Multimodal Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13386)
*Shengchao Chen,Ting Shu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FM$^2$ is a federated foundation model framework for medical imaging that addresses the novel challenge of Imaging Modality Heterogeneity, using dual Mixture-of-Experts modules, Heterogeneous Modality Alignment regularizer with provable convergence guarantees, and Caption-Enhanced Learning via GPT-4o captions to unify training across clients with overlapping or disjoint modalities, achieving state-of-the-art results on classification and medical VQA tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing federated foundation models either fine-tune natural-image models with poor medical-domain transfer or train from scratch within a single modality, lacking the flexibility to unify tasks across heterogeneous medical imaging modalities, especially when clients operate under Overlapped (shared modalities with heterogeneous labels) or Non-overlapped (fully disjoint modalities) regimes.

Method: Proposes FM$^2$ with: (1) backbone trained from scratch for medical domain fidelity, optionally with biomedical pretrained encoders for vision-language alignment; (2) dual Mixture-of-Experts modules (Class-wise MoE for personalized knowledge, Domain-wise MoE for shared cross-modality representations); (3) Heterogeneous Modality Alignment (HMA) regularizer for aligning modality-specific expert parameters with $O(1/\sqrt{T})$ convergence; (4) Caption-Enhanced Learning (CEL) using GPT-4o captions as textual bridges for disjoint modalities; extensible to Federated Medical VQA.

Result: Consistent superiority over state-of-the-art federated baselines and strong out-of-modality generalization on the MIMH benchmark (classification and CEL) and real-world medical VQA datasets across all three tasks.

Conclusion: FM$^2$ effectively addresses Imaging Modality Heterogeneity in federated medical imaging, providing a unified framework with theoretical guarantees and strong empirical performance across classification, caption-enhanced learning, and medical VQA.

Abstract: Building foundation models for medical imaging requires pooling data across institutions, yet privacy regulations prohibit centralized aggregation. Existing Federated Foundation Models either fine-tune natural-image models with poor medical-domain transfer, or train from scratch within a single modality, lacking the flexibility to unify tasks. We identify an under-explored challenge, Imaging Modality Heterogeneity, where clients operate under two structural regimes: Overlapped (shared modalities with heterogeneous label distributions) and Non-overlapped (fully disjoint modalities per client). We propose FM$^2$, a unified framework that trains the core backbone from scratch to preserve medical domain fidelity while optionally incorporating biomedical pretrained encoders for vision-language alignment. FM$^2$ equips each client with dual Mixture-of-Experts modules (a Class-wise MoE for personalized category knowledge and a Domain-wise MoE for shared cross-modality representations), coupled with a Heterogeneous Modality Alignment (HMA) regularizer that explicitly aligns modality-specific expert parameters, admitting provable $O(1/\sqrt{T})$ convergence and generalization guarantees. FM$^2$ further incorporates Caption-Enhanced Learning (CEL), where locally retained GPT-4o-generated captions serve as a textual semantic bridge enabling representation transfer across clients with disjoint modalities, and demonstrates extensibility to Federated Medical VQA. Experiments on our MIMH benchmark (classification and CEL) and real-world medical VQA datasets confirm consistent superiority over state-of-the-art federated baselines and strong out-of-modality generalization across all three tasks.

</details>


### [29] [AnomExpert: Identifying and Selecting Anatomical Planes for Prenatal Ultrasound Anomaly Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13409)
*Jian Wang,Yang Yang,Ziheng Pan,Xiliang Zhu,Yuhan Zhang,Yanfeng Zhou,Dong Ni*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AnomExpert is a prototype-driven framework for prenatal ultrasound anomaly diagnosis that uses learnable plane prototypes and disease-aware sparse selection to model anatomical plane identification and diagnostically relevant plane selection under weak supervision, achieving 86.9% accuracy on a multi-center dataset of 3,654 cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing automated methods for prenatal US anomaly diagnosis either require expensive plane-level annotations or aggregate heterogeneous images without explicitly modeling the critical diagnostic capabilities of identifying anatomical planes and selecting diagnostically relevant planes for each anomaly.

Method: AnomExpert introduces learnable plane prototypes to organize unordered images into latent representations corresponding to anatomical planes without plane annotations, and a disease-aware sparse selection mechanism to select diagnostically relevant planes for each anomaly, using only case-level supervision.

Result: On a multi-center dataset of 3,654 cases, AnomExpert consistently outperformed nine representative multi-instance learning methods. Using a ViT-small backbone, it achieved 86.9% accuracy and 84.2% F1-score while maintaining parameter efficiency.

Conclusion: Modeling anatomical plane identification and disease-specific plane selection improves weakly supervised multi-plane prenatal US anomaly classification.

Abstract: Life-limiting congenital anomalies require accurate prenatal diagnosis for appropriate clinical decision-making. Prenatal ultrasound (US) examinations involve multiple anatomical planes, and diagnosis depends on identifying anatomical planes and selecting diagnostically relevant planes for each anomaly. Existing automated methods either rely on plane-level annotations or aggregate heterogeneous images without explicitly modeling these diagnostic capabilities. We propose AnomExpert, a prototype-driven framework for prenatal US anomaly diagnosis using only case-level supervision. AnomExpert introduces learnable plane prototypes to organize unordered images into latent representations corresponding to anatomical planes without requiring plane annotations. A disease-aware sparse selection mechanism further selects diagnostically relevant planes for each anomaly. Experiments on a multi-center dataset of 3,654 cases show that AnomExpert consistently outperforms nine representative multi-instance learning methods. Using a ViT-small backbone, it achieves 86.9% accuracy and 84.2% F1-score while maintaining parameter efficiency. These findings indicate that modeling anatomical plane identification and disease-specific plane selection improves weakly supervised multi-plane prenatal US anomaly classification. The code is available at https://github.com/TIanCat/AnomExpert.

</details>


### [30] [MultiAnimate: A Unified Framework for Controllable Multi-Character Animation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13415)
*Zhongyi Zhang,Guangyuan Wang,Li Hu,Wenbo Zhou,Peng Zhang,Tianyi Wei,Weiming Zhang,Bang Zhang,Nenghai Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MultiAnimate is a framework for concurrently animating multiple characters from separate reference images while preserving identity consistency and spatial relationships, using an identity-specific reference net, identity-aware pose encoder, and interaction guider module.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing character animation approaches are limited to single reference images, failing to handle scenarios requiring multiple character interaction animation within a shared environment.

Method: (1) Identity-specific reference net for appearance extraction from multiple reference images; (2) Identity-aware pose encoder with attention mechanism for character-pose binding; (3) Interaction guider module using character-specific mask information to refine inter-character interactions.

Result: The framework demonstrates superiority in multiple character animation, especially in complex motion sequences, as verified by extensive experiments and ablation analyses.

Conclusion: MultiAnimate effectively fills the gap in multi-character concurrent animation, enabling identity-consistent and spatially coherent animation from multiple reference images.

Abstract: Recent advances in generative models and technological innovations have significantly addressed the fundamental challenges of character image animation. However, existing approaches predominantly focus on character animation from a single reference image, substantially limiting their applicability in scenarios such as multiple character interaction animation. To fill this gap, this paper introduces MultiAnimate, a comprehensive framework that enables concurrent animation of multiple characters within a shared environment while preserving both identity consistency and spatial relationships. The framework achieves these objectives through multiple well-designed mechanisms. First, we incorporate an identity-specific reference net that enables appearance extraction from multiple reference images, distinguishing MultiAnimate from existing approaches constrained to single reference inputs. Second, we implement an identity-aware pose encoder to address the character-pose binding challenge, wherein an attention mechanism enables the network to accurately differentiate and process multiple pose sequences during generation. Third, we introduce an interaction guider module that enhances the framework's capability to handle complex inter-character interactions by leveraging character-specific mask information, serving as an optional component that refines the pose sequences. Extensive experiments and ablation analyses demonstrate our framework's superiority in multiple character animation, particularly in scenarios involving complex motion sequences.

</details>


### [31] [ScanFocus: A Coarse-to-Fine Framework for Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13421)
*Kai Chen,Ming Dai,Wenxuan Cheng,Wankou Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ScanFocus is a coarse-to-fine framework for Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding that decouples the task into global spatio-temporal scanning and local boundary focusing, using a Deformable Semantic-Motion Fusion module for coarse proposals and a Semantic-Guided Temporal Aggregator for precise boundary refinement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing STVG methods struggle to balance global context modeling with precise boundary localization due to low-rate temporal downsampling and implicit motion modeling, which suppress high-frequency boundary cues and neglect explicit inter-frame dependencies.

Method: ScanFocus decouples STVG into two stages: (1) a global spatio-temporal scan using a unified vision-language fusion encoder and a lightweight Deformable Semantic-Motion Fusion module for coarse proposal generation; (2) a local boundary focus stage employing a Semantic-Guided Temporal Aggregator that densely samples around coarse boundaries and explicitly models short-term temporal interactions under semantic guidance for precise timestamp regression.

Result: Extensive experiments on three widely used benchmarks demonstrate performance superiority over previous approaches.

Conclusion: The coarse-to-fine decoupling strategy effectively addresses the trade-off between global context and precise boundary localization in STVG, enabling better handling of rapid motion changes and fine-grained boundary details.

Abstract: Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding (STVG) aims to retrieve the visual trajectory of a specific object from a video stream as described by a natural language expression. However, most advanced methods struggle to balance global context modeling with precise boundary localization. Due to the prohibitive computational costs of processing long videos, these approaches typically resort to low-rate temporal downsampling and implicit motion modeling. This inevitably suppresses high-frequency boundary cues and neglects the explicit inter-frame dependencies required for precise boundary delineation. To address these limitations, we present \textbf{ScanFocus}, a novel coarse-to-fine framework that decouples the STVG task into a global spatio-temporal scan and a local boundary focus. Specifically, we utilize a unified vision-language fusion encoder combined with a lightweight Deformable Semantic-Motion Fusion module to efficiently align multimodal features and generate coarse proposals. To recover the suppressed fine-grained details, we introduce the Semantic-Guided Temporal Aggregator (SGTA) in the refinement stage. By densely sampling around coarse boundaries, SGTA explicitly models short-term temporal interactions under semantic guidance, capturing rapid motion changes for precise timestamp regression. Extensive experiments on three widely used benchmarks demonstrate the performance superiority of our proposed method over previous approaches. Code will be released at https://github.com/TenMinutes209/ScanFocus.

</details>


### [32] [CLIP-Guided Label-Free Discriminative Region Scoring for Fine-Grained Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13437)
*Yujie Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified CLIP-guided label-free region scoring framework for fine-grained classification, comparing SAM masks and random crops under various scoring strategies. Findings show Soft Negative Margin scoring is best, and random-crop-based pseudo-label scoring outperforms SAM-based methods due to more stable semantic context.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: It remains unclear how different local regions and CLIP-based scoring strategies affect discriminative evidence selection for fine-grained classification when ground-truth labels are unavailable.

Method: A unified CLIP-guided label-free region scoring framework for fine-grained classification, evaluating cosine similarity-based, margin-based, and entropy-based scoring strategies with SAM-generated masks and random crops, along with two pseudo-label variants based on global and local region embeddings.

Result: Soft Negative Margin scoring is the best; pseudo-label scoring approximates true-label performance; random-crop-based pseudo-label scoring outperforms SAM-based scoring; SAM masks benefit from full aggregation while random crops prefer top-k selection.

Conclusion: Soft Negative Margin scoring achieves the strongest performance; pseudo-label scoring closely approximates true-label performance; random-crop-based pseudo-label scoring consistently outperforms SAM-based scoring, as random crops preserve surrounding information and provide more stable semantic context under noisy pseudo-labels; SAM masks benefit from aggregating all region embeddings, while random crops perform better with a smaller top-k subset.

Abstract: Recent vision models such as CLIP and SAM enable training-free segmentation and semantic encoding for fine-grained classification. A common approach is to compare the representations of segmented image regions with the text prompt embeddings of the corresponding labels. However, it remains unclear how different local regions and CLIP-based scoring strategies affect the selection of discriminative evidence, especially when ground-truth labels are unavailable. In this paper, we propose a unified CLIP-guided label-free region scoring framework for fine-grained classification. The framework evaluates cosine similarity-based, margin-based, and entropy-based scoring strategies using both SAM-generated masks and random crops, and introduces two label-free pseudo-label variants based on global image embeddings and local region embeddings. We conduct experiments on five fine-grained classification datasets to systematically compare different region generation methods and scoring strategies. The results show that Soft Negative Margin scoring achieves the strongest performance, and pseudo-label scoring closely approximates true-label performance. Although SAM produces semantically meaningful masks, random-crop-based pseudo-label scoring consistently outperforms SAM-based scoring across all datasets, suggesting that random crops preserve surrounding information and provide more stable semantic context when pseudo-labels are noisy. In addition, SAM masks benefit from aggregating embeddings from all regions, whereas random crops tend to perform better with a smaller top-k subset. These findings provide new insights for fine-grained classification.

</details>


### [33] [DreamSat-Pose: Spacecraft Pose Estimation from Single-View 3D Reconstructions and Learned 2D-3D Feature Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13449)
*Josiane Uwumukiza,Jocelyn Zhao,Giovanni Lavezzi,Giacomo Battaglia,Paolo Panicucci,Minduli C. Wijayatunga,Victor Rodriguez-Fernandez,Richard Linares*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel framework for single-shot shape reconstruction and 6-DoF pose estimation of unknown spacecraft from a single image, using DINOv3 features, dynamic graph CNN, dual-stream transformer matcher, and PnP solver, achieving 0.157° mean pointing error on the SPE3R dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 6-DoF pose estimation is critical for autonomous rendezvous and proximity operations, but becomes challenging when the target is unknown because it must be paired with target shape model reconstruction.

Method: (1) Frozen DINOv3 vision transformer for image feature extraction; (2) Trainable dynamic graph convolutional neural network for geometric features from reconstructed point cloud; (3) Dual-stream transformer matcher with alternating self- and cross-attention to refine descriptors and produce soft 2D-3D correspondences; (4) Perspective-n-Point solver for pose recovery.

Result: Achieves 0.157 degrees mean pointing error using only a single image and reconstructed geometry on the SPE3R dataset, demonstrating strong generalization to unseen spacecraft.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides reliable single-shot pose estimates for unknown spacecraft without prior knowledge of the target shape, outperforming the FoundationPose baseline.

Abstract: 6-DoF pose estimation is a critical task in autonomous rendezvous and proximity operations. In the case of an unknown target, this task becomes challenging as it shall be paired with the reconstruction of the target shape model. In this article, we propose a novel framework for single-shot shape and pose estimation of unknown spacecraft objects. Given a single image, we first reconstruct a 3D shape model of the target, then estimate the relative six-degrees-of-freedom pose by learning dense 2D-3D correspondences. The image features are extracted using a frozen DINOv3 vision transformer, while the geometric features are computed from the reconstructed point cloud using a trainable dynamic graph convolutional neural network encoder. A dual-stream transformer matcher refines descriptors through alternating self- and cross-attention, producing soft correspondences that are passed to a Perspective-$n$-Point solver for pose recovery. We evaluate the method on the SPE3R dataset and consider FoundationPose as a representative baseline for current state-of-the-art capabilities. Results show reliable pose estimates achieving 0.157 degrees mean pointing error using only a single image and reconstructed geometry, demonstrating strong generalization to unseen spacecraft.

</details>


### [34] [Symbiosis-Inspired Knowledge Distillation for Incremental Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13452)
*Mingyue Zeng,De Cheng,Zhipeng Xu,Huaijie Wang,Nannan Wang,Xinbo Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes Symbiosis-Inspired Knowledge Distillation (SIKD), a method for incremental object detection that leverages object symbiosis through spatial and semantic distillation to preserve shared representations and mitigate catastrophic forgetting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing IOD methods adopt a separation-oriented paradigm that isolates feature spaces for new and old classes, which overlooks object symbiosis (co-occurrence and occlusion) in detection. Ignoring these spatial and semantic dependencies distorts shared representations, exacerbates confusion between old and new classes, and accelerates catastrophic forgetting.

Method: SIKD operates at two complementary levels: (1) Spatial Symbiosis Distillation (SpSD) identifies symbiotic regions with high overlap between old model responses and new task objects, preserves generalizable old class cues while suppressing bias/redundancy, and distills refined evidence to the new model with slot-aligned supervision. (2) Semantic Symbiosis Distillation (SeSD) maintains class-level structure by forming confidence-weighted prototypes for old classes and aligning their inter-class soft ranks over old class logits to stabilize semantic topology.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness and superiority of the proposed SIKD method for incremental object detection.

Conclusion: Leveraging object symbiosis explicitly through spatial and semantic knowledge distillation effectively preserves shared representations and mitigates catastrophic forgetting in incremental object detection, outperforming separation-oriented approaches.

Abstract: Incremental object detection (IOD) aims to extend detectors to new categories while retaining previously acquired knowledge. Existing methods often adopt a class incremental learning perspective, separating feature spaces to sharpen decision boundaries. However, this separation-oriented paradigm may overlook object symbiosis in detection, where co-occurrence and occlusion introduce spatial and semantic dependencies that benefit from shared representations. Ignoring these dependencies distorts the shared representations, exacerbates confusion between old and new classes, and accelerates catastrophic forgetting. To address this, we propose Symbiosis-Inspired Knowledge Distillation (SIKD), which explicitly leverages object symbiosis at two complementary levels. Spatial Symbiosis Distillation (SpSD) focuses on symbiotic regions where the old model responds with high overlap to objects in the new task. It preserves generalizable old class cues, suppresses class-specific bias and redundancy, and distills the refined evidence to the new model at matched spatial locations with slot-aligned supervision. Semantic Symbiosis Distillation (SeSD) maintains class level structure by forming confidence weighted prototypes for old classes and aligning their inter class soft ranks over the old class logits, which stabilizes the semantic topology during adaptation. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness and superiority of the proposed method.

</details>


### [35] [GeoAnchor: Collaborative Reasoning via Latent Decomposition for 3D Spatial Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13454)
*Hao Li,Han Fang,Zixin Pan,Xin Wei,Hongbo Sun,Jinglin Xu,Zhiyu Lin,Ye Yuan,Zhongjiang He,Yu Yu,Hao Sun*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GeoAnchor is an interleaved text-latent reasoning framework for MLLMs that decomposes 3D spatial information into position, direction, and geometry latents to improve spatial reasoning from 2D images, outperforming state-of-the-art methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MLLMs struggle with 3D spatial understanding from 2D images because symbolic text tokens lack fidelity for continuous geometry, and single-latent methods fail to adapt to diverse spatial tasks, causing misalignment in complex scenarios.

Method: GeoAnchor decomposes 3D spatial information into three complementary latent types: position latents (object grounding), direction latents (relational orientation), and geometry latents (scene structure). These are recombined in a structured space to construct local evidence while capturing global context. A collaborative training strategy guides the model from local spatial perception to comprehensive 3D understanding.

Result: Extensive experiments on diverse and complex 3D reasoning tasks show GeoAnchor outperforms state-of-the-art methods, demonstrating effectiveness and strong generalization capabilities.

Conclusion: GeoAnchor's multi-latent, interleaved text-latent reasoning framework effectively addresses misalignment in 3D spatial reasoning for MLLMs, offering a dynamic and interpretable alternative to single-latent or pure text-token approaches.

Abstract: Although multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved remarkable progress, understanding 3D spatial relationships from 2D images remains a critical challenge. Existing methods primarily rely on symbolic text tokens, which inherently lack the fidelity to represent continuous geometric information. While recent methods use latent representations to enhance reasoning, relying on a single latent type cannot adapt to the diversity of spatial tasks, leading to misalignment in complex geometric scenarios. To address these limitations, we propose GeoAnchor, an interleaved text-latent reasoning framework. GeoAnchor decomposes 3D spatial information into three complementary components: position latents for object grounding, direction latents for relational orientation, and geometry latents for scene structure. These components are recombined in a structured space to construct local evidence while capturing global context, enabling dynamic and interpretable reasoning. Furthermore, we introduce a collaborative training strategy that guides the model from local spatial perception to comprehensive 3D understanding. Extensive experiments on diverse and complex 3D reasoning tasks demonstrate that GeoAnchor outperforms the state of the art, validating its effectiveness and generalization capabilities.

</details>


### [36] [2D Rotary Position Embedding for Scene Text Recognition with Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13458)
*Zobeir Raisi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 2D-RoPE-STR adapts axial 2D Rotary Position Embedding to scene text recognition by using anisotropic dimension allocation matching text aspect ratios and extending rotary coupling to encoder-decoder cross-attention, improving performance on curved, rotated, and perspective-distorted text with minimal architectural changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing Transformer-based STR methods use 1D positional encodings that ignore 2D spatial structure, and prior 2D extensions of RoPE assume square/isotropic images and only apply within encoder self-attention, neither of which suits scene text (anisotropic crops, encoder-decoder architecture).

Method: (1) Anisotropic row/column dimension allocation in axial 2D-RoPE matched to text aspect ratio; (2) Extension of rotary coupling into encoder-decoder cross-attention for 2D layout-aware decoding; both are parameter-free and require no architectural redesign beyond the positional encoding module.

Result: Gains concentrate on irregular layouts (curved, rotated, perspective-distorted) across six benchmarks (IIIT5K, SVT, ICDAR 2013/2015, CUTE80, SVTP), validated via controlled ablation pair isolating positional encoding, image-level net-win disagreement analysis, and encoder attention visualization.

Conclusion: 2D-RoPE-STR effectively encodes 2D spatial structure for scene text recognition with minimal overhead, outperforming 1D RoPE and 2D sinusoidal/learnable alternatives specifically on geometrically challenging text.

Abstract: Scene Text Recognition (STR) remains challenging due to the diversity of text appearances, including curvature, rotation, and perspective distortion. Recent Transformer-based approaches perform well but usually rely on one-dimensional positional encodings that ignore the 2D spatial structure of text images. Axial 2D extensions of Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE) exist for vision Transformers, but they assume roughly square, isotropic image content and apply the rotation only within encoder self-attention. Scene text violates both assumptions: crops are markedly anisotropic, and STR models are encoder-decoder, so the decoder must relate its queries to the encoder's 2D layout through cross-attention. We introduce 2D-RoPE-STR, which adapts axial 2D-RoPE to this setting through (1) an anisotropic row/column dimension allocation matched to the aspect ratio of text, and (2) an extension of the rotary coupling into encoder-decoder cross-attention, letting autoregressive decoding steps attend to encoder tokens by their 2D layout, a setting not addressed by prior encoder-only formulations. Both changes are essentially parameter-free and require no architectural redesign beyond the positional-encoding module. We further introduce a diagnostic protocol (a controlled ablation pair isolating only the positional encoding, an image-level net-win disagreement analysis, and encoder attention visualization) that identifies where and why relative 2D position helps: curved, rotated, and perspective-distorted layouts where reading order departs from a straight horizontal line. On six standard benchmarks (IIIT5K, SVT, ICDAR 2013, ICDAR 2015, CUTE80, SVTP), gains concentrate on exactly these irregular layouts, with ablations isolating each design choice against 1D RoPE and 2D sinusoidal and learnable alternatives.

</details>


### [37] [LPM: Industrial-Scale Generative Video Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13460)
*Bichuan Zhu,Fulin Li,Jiachao Gong,Jinhua Hao,Kai Zhao,Kun Yuan,Pengcheng Xu,Qiang Wang,Qiao Mo,Yanlong Yuan,Yizhen Shao,Yuxiao Hu,Zixi Tuo,Ming Sun,Chao Zhou,Bin Chen,Bin Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LPM is the first industrially deployed diffusion-based generative video restoration model, handling diverse in-the-wild degradations at scale. Deployed at Kuaishou, it processes ~45% of total viewing time, reduces bitrate by 20% at comparable quality, and saves hundreds of millions in bandwidth costs annually.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for a practical, scalable, and cost-effective generative video restoration system capable of handling diverse, complex degradations in user-generated content (UGC) at industrial scale, as existing methods were not deployed in production.

Method: A unified diffusion-based framework with: (1) large-scale data engineering, (2) enhanced architecture with progressive training strategy, and (3) temporal-pyramid inference mechanism for processing arbitrarily long videos with temporal consistency.

Result: Deployed at Kuaishou, accounting for ~45% of total viewing time; reduces bitrate by 20% against Kuaishou's in-house codec at comparable perceptual quality; yields annual bandwidth cost savings of hundreds of millions; integrated into Kling product.

Conclusion: Generative video restoration can be practical, scalable, and cost-effective for large-scale deployment, delivering both perceptual enhancement and substantial system-level benefits in bandwidth reduction and cost savings.

Abstract: We present the Large Processing Model (LPM), a diffusion-based generative framework for photorealistic video restoration under complex, in-the-wild degradations. To our knowledge, LPM is the first generative video restoration model deployed at industrial scale. LPM addresses the diverse degradations in user-generated content (UGC) through a unified system encompassing large-scale data engineering, foundation-model training, and efficient inference. Its enhanced architecture, progressive training strategy, and temporal-pyramid inference mechanism jointly enable high-fidelity, temporally consistent restoration of arbitrarily long videos across the broad content distribution encountered on UGC platforms. LPM has been deployed in production at Kuaishou, where videos processed by the model account for approximately 45% of total viewing time, delivering consistent improvements across key quality-of-experience metrics. Beyond perceptual enhancement, LPM delivers substantial system-level benefits: at comparable perceptual quality, it reduces bitrate by 20% relative to Kuaishou's in-house codec, yielding annual bandwidth cost savings on the order of hundreds of millions. Its low serving cost also enables integration into products such as Kling, demonstrating that generative restoration can be practical, scalable, and cost-effective for large-scale video processing.

</details>


### [38] [HIVE-3D: Hierarchical Voxel Enhancement for High-Quality 3D Scene Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13468)
*Bin Zang,Wenting Zheng,Xiaoliang Luo,Zhiyuan Fang,Shi Li,Lvchun Wang,Wei Yu,Yi Zhao,Tian Xie,Yuchi Huo,Rengan Xie*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HIVE-3D introduces a hierarchical voxel enhancement framework for generating high-quality 3D scenes from a single image, using image segmentation, attention-based retrieval, a hierarchical component tree, and a voxel super-resolution model to achieve coarse-to-fine high-resolution 3D scene generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods can generate impressive 3D objects from a single image but are limited by restricted representation resolution, making them unsuitable for 3D scene generation.

Method: 1) Coarse initial scene generation from a single image; 2) Image segmentation and attention-based retrieval to align 2D components with 3D scene components; 3) Organization of scene relations into a hierarchical component tree; 4) A voxel super-resolution model for refined voxel generation consistent with coarse voxels; 5) Coarse-to-fine hierarchical super-resolution on images and voxels per component.

Result: The method significantly outperforms previous approaches, achieving state-of-the-art performance in 3D scene generation from a single image.

Conclusion: HIVE-3D enables high-resolution, high-quality 3D scene generation from a single image by leveraging hierarchical voxel enhancement, overcoming the resolution limitations of prior 3D object-centric methods.

Abstract: Recently, a line of works can generate impressive 3D objects from a single image, but they are limited by restricted representation resolution, making them unsuitable for 3D scene generation. In this work, we introduce HIVE-3D, a novel method for high-quality 3D scene generation based on hierarchical voxel enhancement framework. Specifically, given a single scene image as input, we first produce a coarse initial scene, then introduce image segmentation and attention-based retrieval to align 2D image components with 3D scene components. Subsequently, we organize these scene relations into a hierarchical component tree, where nodes closer to the leaves denote finer-grained components. Finally, we propose a voxel super-resolution model that generates refined voxels for the target instance while maintaining strong consistency with the coarse voxels. Equipped with this model, we perform coarse-to-fine hierarchical super-resolution on images and voxels for each component, producing a high-resolution and high-quality 3D scene. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method significantly outperforms previous approaches, achieving state-of-the-art performance.

</details>


### [39] [GPOcc++: Unified Sparse Gaussian Occupancy Prediction with Visual Geometry Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13481)
*Changqing Zhou,Yueru Luo,Yulan Guo,Bing Wang,Jie Qin,Changhao Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GPOcc/GPOcc++ transforms visual geometry priors into occupancy-aware sparse Gaussian representations for efficient 3D scene understanding, handling multi-view and temporal data in a unified framework, achieving strong performance on indoor and outdoor benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate 3D scene understanding is fundamental for embodied intelligence and autonomous driving. While 3D occupancy provides a unified representation, recovering volumetric information from visual observations is challenging, especially in occluded and unobserved regions. Visual geometry priors offer strong cues but are surface-centric, whereas occupancy prediction requires reasoning about volumetric interiors and free space.

Method: GPOcc transforms visual geometry priors into occupancy-aware sparse Gaussian representations for volumetric scene modeling. GPOcc++ extends this by modeling multi-view observations and temporal sequences within a unified framework, using the same representation for spatial and temporal evidence. The approach is further extended from indoor to outdoor occupancy prediction.

Result: Extensive experiments on both indoor and outdoor benchmarks demonstrate consistently strong performance across multi-view and temporal settings, along with favorable efficiency and generalization.

Conclusion: The proposed Gaussian-based representation effectively bridges the gap between surface-centric geometry priors and volumetric occupancy prediction, providing a unified, efficient, and generalizable solution for 3D scene understanding.

Abstract: Accurate 3D scene understanding is fundamental to embodied intelligence and autonomous driving, where 3D occupancy provides a unified representation of objects, structures, and free space. However, recovering such a complete volumetric representation from visual observations remains challenging, particularly in occluded and unobserved regions. Visual geometry priors offer strong and generalizable geometric cues for addressing this challenge, but their outputs are inherently surface-centric, whereas occupancy prediction requires reasoning about volumetric interiors and free space. To bridge this gap, we introduce GPOcc, which transforms visual geometry priors into occupancy-aware sparse Gaussian representations for efficient and expressive volumetric scene modeling. Building on GPOcc, GPOcc++ models multi-view observations and temporal sequences within a unified framework, allowing spatial and temporal evidence to be handled through the same representation. We further extend GPOcc++ from indoor scenes to outdoor occupancy prediction. Extensive experiments on both indoor and outdoor benchmarks demonstrate consistently strong performance across both multi-view and temporal settings, together with favorable efficiency and generalization. Code will be released at https://github.com/JuIvyy/GPOcc.

</details>


### [40] [M2P-AD: Memory-to-Prototype Learning with Boundary-aware Score Refinement for 3D Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13499)
*Seyoung Jeong,Jong Pil Yun,Sang Jun Lee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: M2P-AD introduces a Memory-to-Prototype module and Boundary-aware score refinement to suppress false positives near object boundaries and excessive anomaly scores in normal regions, achieving state-of-the-art 3D anomaly detection on Real3D-AD, Anomaly-ShapeNet, and MulSen-AD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D anomaly detection methods suffer from excessive anomaly responses in normal regions and false positives near object boundaries, limiting their reliability in real-world industrial environments.

Method: M2P-AD consists of three components: (1) Memory-to-Prototype (M2P) module that learns representative prototypes from normal feature embeddings to preserve structural information; (2) Boundary extraction (BE) module to identify object boundaries; (3) Boundary-aware score refinement (BSR) strategy that recalibrates anomaly scores by incorporating boundary characteristics.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on Real3D-AD, Anomaly-ShapeNet, and MulSen-AD benchmarks. Qualitative results show reduced false positives near boundaries and suppressed excessive anomaly scores in normal regions.

Conclusion: The proposed approach enables more reliable 3D anomaly detection and provides a robust solution applicable to real-world industrial environments.

Abstract: 3D anomaly detection has recently emerged as an important research topic in computer vision. Although existing methods have achieved high performance, excessive anomaly responses in normal regions and false positives near object boundaries remain unresolved challenges. To address these challenges, we propose a novel 3D anomaly detection model, Memory-to-Prototype Anomaly Detection (M2P-AD), which effectively models the distribution of normal features while suppressing excessive anomaly scores in normal regions and false positives near object boundaries. Specifically, we introduce a Memory-to-Prototype (M2P) module that learns representative prototypes from normal feature embeddings to preserve important structural information of objects. In addition, a Boundary extraction (BE) module is integrated to identify object boundaries, and a Boundary-aware score refinement (BSR) strategy is applied to recalibrate anomaly scores by incorporating boundary characteristics. The proposed method is evaluated on Real3D-AD, Anomaly-ShapeNet, and MulSen-AD, achieving state-of-the-art performance. Qualitative results demonstrate that excessive anomaly scores in normal regions are reduced and false positives near object boundaries are suppressed, resulting in more accurate and stable anomaly localization. The results indicate that the proposed approach enables more reliable 3D anomaly detection and provides a robust solution applicable to real-world industrial environments.

</details>


### [41] [Attention-Free and Lightweight Token Reduction for Efficient Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13500)
*Xuanyi Hao,Zuoyuan Zhang,Zhibo Wang,Xiaoyi Pang,Jiahui Hu,Jiacheng Du,Shuguo Zhuo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes an attention-free, lightweight token reduction framework for Vision-Language Models that uses entropy-based importance estimation and transformation-induced consistency signals to select important and diverse visual tokens, achieving efficient inference on edge devices without relying on attention maps or costly pairwise comparisons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VLMs are difficult to deploy on resource-constrained edge devices due to high computational overhead from numerous visual tokens. Existing token reduction methods are either incompatible with modern acceleration frameworks (attention-map-dependent) or computationally expensive (pairwise similarity comparisons), limiting scalability and practical deployment.

Method: A plug-and-play token reduction framework with two components: (1) entropy-based importance estimation (information-theoretic) that selects tokens with expressive, non-degenerate features; (2) transformation-induced consistency signals where sorting by this signal groups similar tokens together, enabling stride-based selection for diverse token coverage without pairwise comparisons.

Result: Extensive experiments across multiple VLM benchmarks show the framework achieves a favorable accuracy-efficiency trade-off, maintaining competitive performance even under aggressive token compression.

Conclusion: The proposed attention-free, lightweight token reduction framework enables efficient VLM inference on edge devices while preserving performance, outperforming existing approaches in scalability and practical deployability.

Abstract: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have achieved strong performance in multimodal understanding, yet remain challenging to deploy on resource-constrained edge devices due to the substantial computational overhead of processing numerous visual tokens. Token reduction is a promising direction for accelerating VLMs inference, but existing approaches either rely on attention maps that are incompatible with modern acceleration frameworks or depend on computationally intensive pairwise similarity comparisons, which undermine scalability and negate their practical benefits in deployment. In this paper, we propose an attention-free and lightweight token reduction framework as a plug-and-play module for VLMs, which preserves both important and diverse tokens to produce a compact visual representation. First, to enable attention-free importance estimation, we adopt an information-theoretic perspective and quantify token information using a novel entropy-based criterion, retaining those with more expressive and less degenerate feature representations. Second, to ensure diverse visual coverage in a lightweight manner, we introduce a transformation-induced consistency signal where similar tokens yield similar signals, such that sorting by this signal places similar tokens close to each other and enables stride-based selection to produce a diverse token set. Extensive experiments across multiple VLMs benchmarks demonstrate that our framework achieves a favorable accuracy-efficiency trade-off, maintaining competitive performance under aggressive compression.

</details>


### [42] [DP-BOA: Dirichlet-Process Birth-or-Assign for On-the-Fly Category Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13504)
*Peiyan Gu,Zixin Teng,Xuming He*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DP-BOA is a posterior-predictive decision framework for on-the-fly category discovery that uses an online Dirichlet-process Gaussian mixture model to explicitly compare evidence for assigning a test sample to an existing category versus spawning a new one, outperforming baselines on OCD benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing on-the-fly category discovery methods treat category birth implicitly as a fallback when no existing category matches confidently, lacking explicit statistical evidence for spawning new categories.

Method: Proposes DP-BOA, an online Dirichlet-process Gaussian mixture model with a Normal-Inverse-Wishart prior. Uses labeled data to calibrate a shared NIW prior and warm-start known-category posteriors. At test time, compares posterior predictive evidence for assignment to existing categories vs. spawning a new category via the DP prior, then updates statistics online.

Result: Consistently outperforms strong baselines across standard OCD benchmarks, delivering particularly strong novel-class discovery performance while maintaining competitive known-class accuracy.

Conclusion: DP-BOA provides a principled statistical framework for explicit category birth decisions, capturing anisotropic category geometry and adapting decision confidence as evidence accumulates.

Abstract: On-the-fly category discovery requires deciding for each incoming test sample whether to assign it to an existing category or spawn a new one. Existing methods typically implement this decision through matching-based heuristics, such as radius- or hash-based rules. While effective in practice, these methods usually treat category birth implicitly as a fallback when no existing category matches confidently, rather than as an explicit alternative supported by its own statistical evidence. To address this, we propose DP-BOA, a posterior-predictive decision framework based on an online Dirichlet-process Gaussian mixture model with a Normal-Inverse-Wishart prior. During training, we use labeled data to calibrate a shared NIW prior over category Gaussians and warm-start the known-category posteriors. At test time, for each incoming sample, DP-BOA compares the posterior predictive evidence for assignment to existing categories against the evidence for spawning a new category induced by the DP prior, and then updates category statistics online after the decision. The method captures anisotropic category geometry and naturally adapts decision confidence as evidence accumulates. Across standard OCD benchmarks, DP-BOA consistently outperforms strong baselines and delivers particularly strong novel-class discovery performance while maintaining competitive known-class accuracy.

</details>


### [43] [TRACE-PCa: Predicting Prostate Cancer Progression from Longitudinal MRI During Active Surveillance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13506)
*Hongye Zeng,Shreeram Athreya,Dingyuan Dai,Steve Raman,Leonard Marks,William Speier,Corey Arnold*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes an end-to-end temporal multimodal model that uses a pretrained 3D MRI foundation model with a temporal attention gate to predict pathological progression during active surveillance for prostate cancer, without requiring lesion segmentation, outperforming baselines and matching radiologist performance while reducing unnecessary biopsies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current active surveillance protocols rely on scheduled repeat biopsies, most of which are unnecessary, and existing risk-stratification tools are limited to single time-point imaging or require explicit lesion segmentation, excluding many patients.

Method: A pretrained 3D MRI foundation model encodes serial scans; a temporal attention gate recalibrates multi-visit features to amplify progression-related changes; gated imaging features are fused with clinical variables in a multimodal framework to estimate progression probability.

Result: The approach consistently outperforms competing baselines, performs comparably to radiologist assessment, maintains high negative predictive value, and achieves higher positive predictive value, demonstrating potential to safely reduce unnecessary biopsies.

Conclusion: The proposed temporal multimodal model can safely reduce unnecessary biopsies during active surveillance by accurately predicting pathological progression without requiring lesion segmentation or single time-point imaging.

Abstract: Active surveillance (AS) is the preferred strategy for favorable-risk prostate cancer, yet current protocols rely on scheduled repeat biopsies, most of which reveal no progression and are unnecessary. Existing risk-stratification tools operate on single time-point imaging or depend on explicit lesion segmentation, limiting their ability to capture longitudinal change and excluding patients without an MRI-visible lesion. In this study, we propose an end-to-end temporal and multimodal model for predicting pathological progression during AS without lesion segmentation. We encode each serial scan with a pretrained 3D MRI foundation model and introduce a temporal attention gate that recalibrates the multi-visit features to amplify focal imaging changes associated with progression. The gated imaging representation is then fused with clinical variables in a multimodal framework to estimate the probability of progression. Validated on a longitudinal AS cohort, our approach consistently outperforms competing baselines and performs comparably to the radiologist assessment representing current clinical practice. It maintains high negative predictive value while achieving higher positive predictive value, demonstrating its potential to safely reduce unnecessary biopsies during surveillance.

</details>


### [44] [DriveFace: A Cross-Spectral Through-Glass Face Dataset for On-the-Move Vehicular Border Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13515)
*Anjith George,Luis Luevano,Alain Komaty,Zeina Al Amine,Vidit Vidit,Sebastien Marcel*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new dataset for on-the-move face recognition in border control, using NIR vehicle-crossing videos paired with smartphone pre-enrollment data, and shows that current state-of-the-art models perform poorly under these realistic conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing face recognition benchmarks are collected in controlled environments and fail to capture challenges specific to vehicular acquisition (motion blur, variable illumination, occlusions, cross-spectral enrollment), hindering progress in on-the-move biometric authentication for border control.

Method: The authors construct a dataset of NIR vehicle-crossing videos paired with smartphone-based pre-enrollment data, and conduct baseline evaluations using state-of-the-art face recognition models.

Result: State-of-the-art models show clear performance limitations under these realistic vehicular conditions, indicating that current methods are insufficient for this task.

Conclusion: There is a need for dedicated methods to advance on-the-move face recognition in border-control scenarios, as existing models struggle with the challenges present in this dataset.

Abstract: The continuous growth in cross-border mobility places increasing pressure on existing border control infrastructures, motivating on-the-move biometric authentication, in which travellers are identified directly inside their vehicles at checkpoints. Face recognition is well-suited to this setting, as it can be acquired passively and at a distance. Its development, however, is hindered by the lack of representative datasets: existing benchmarks are collected in controlled environments and do not capture the challenges inherent to vehicular acquisition, including motion blur, variable illumination, occlusions, and cross-spectral enrollment. To address this gap, we introduce a dataset for on-the-move face recognition in border-control scenarios, comprising NIR vehicle-crossing videos paired with smartphone-based pre-enrollment data. Baseline evaluations with state-of-the-art models show clear performance limitations under these realistic conditions, highlighting the need for dedicated methods to advance the field.

</details>


### [45] [VGIF-Score: Interpretable and Diagnostic Evaluation of Spatio-Temporal Instruction Following in Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13527)
*Songyu Xu,Xin Wang,Qiang Chen,Xinran Wang,Muxi Diao,Yuxuan Zhang,Kongming Liang,Rui Lin,Zhanyu Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes VGIF-Score, an automated and interpretable evaluation framework for instruction following in video generation, comprising an objective completion branch (ST-DAG parsing with dependency-aware QA) and a subjective satisfaction branch (AutoRubric-based assessment), instantiated on the VGIF-Bench benchmark of 223 complex prompts with ~4.3K evaluation items, tested on 14 VGMs across 3K+ videos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing video generation evaluation protocols rely on short, semantically shallow prompts with limited constraints and weak spatio-temporal dependencies, often depending on costly human evaluation or handcrafted pipelines while providing little diagnostic insight into which instruction constraints succeed or fail.

Method: VGIF-Score has two components: (1) objective completion branch that parses prompts into Spatio-Temporal Directed Acyclic Graphs (ST-DAG) and performs dependency-aware QA with short-circuit diagnostics; (2) subjective satisfaction branch using instruction-conditioned AutoRubric to assess cinematography, visual purity, motion smoothness, and physics adherence.

Result: Experiments on 14 proprietary and open-source VGMs across 3K+ generated videos show that VGIF-Score provides reliable, interpretable, and diagnostically useful evaluation of video generation instruction following.

Conclusion: VGIF-Score offers a highly automated and interpretable solution for evaluating instruction following in video generation, addressing the lack of diagnostic insights in existing benchmarks.

Abstract: Recent video generation models (VGMs) have made substantial progress in visual fidelity, yet their ability to follow long, compositional instructions remains insufficiently evaluated. Existing evaluation protocols often rely on prompts that are short and semantically shallow, with limited atomic constraints and weak spatio-temporal dependencies. They also frequently depend on costly human evaluation or handcrafted vision pipelines, while providing little diagnostic insight into which instruction constraints succeed or fail. To address this gap, we propose VGIF-Score, a highly automated and interpretable framework for evaluating instruction following in video generation. VGIF-Score consists of two complementary components: an objective completion branch that parses prompts into a Spatio-Temporal Directed Acyclic Graph (ST-DAG) and performs dependency-aware QA with short-circuit diagnostics, and a subjective satisfaction branch that uses instruction-conditioned AutoRubric to assess cinematography, visual purity, motion smoothness, and physics adherence. Together, these components produce a unified score that captures both objective completion and perceptual satisfaction. We instantiate this framework on VGIF-Bench, a benchmark of 223 long, structurally entangled prompts paired with approximately 4.3K fine-grained evaluation items. Experiments on 14 proprietary and open-source VGMs across more than 3K generated videos show that VGIF-Score provides reliable, interpretable, and diagnostically useful evaluation of video generation instruction following. The code will be available at https://github.com/PRIS-CV/VGIF-SCORE.

</details>


### [46] [Nexus: Native Mesh Generation with Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13563)
*Hanxiao Wang,Ying-Tian Liu,Yuan-Chen Guo,Qi-Yuan Feng,Zi-Xin Zou,Ding Liang,Biao Zhang,Yan-Pei Cao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Nexus is a diffusion-based method for holistic mesh generation that decouples vertex and topology generation, outperforming autoregressive and two-stage baselines on Objaverse and Toys4K datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mainstream autoregressive mesh generation methods struggle with effective inference and are sensitive to error accumulation.

Method: (1) Vertex generation: views mesh vertices as sparse voxels in an octree and uses a diffusion model for coarse-to-fine vertex generation. (2) Topology modeling: proposes Spacetime Interval, an extension of Spacetime Distance, to encode edge/face topology into continuous per-vertex embeddings, then uses a diffusion model to generate these embeddings on the generated vertices.

Result: Outperforms state-of-the-art autoregressive and two-stage baselines on Objaverse and Toys4K datasets; blind user study from 3D practitioners confirms strong perceptual preference.

Conclusion: Nexus effectively circumvents limitations of sequential mesh modeling via decoupled diffusion-based vertex and topology generation.

Abstract: Generating high-quality triangle meshes is essential for film, gaming, and interactive 3D applications. Mainstream methods rely on mesh serialization and autoregressive processes, which stuggles in effective inference and is sensitive to error accumulation. In this paper, we present Nexus, a diffusion method that achieves holistic mesh generation via decoupled vertex and topology generation. First, we view mesh vertices as sparse voxels organized as an octree and adopt a diffusion model to generate the vertices in a coarse-to-fine manner. Second, for topology modeling, we propose Spacetime Interval, as an extension of Spacetime Distance to encode arbitrary edge and face topology into continuous per-vertex embeddings. It allows for a global and efficient recovery of complex topology. We then employ a diffusion model to generate the continuous embeddings on the generated vertices. Extensive experiments on the Objaverse and Toys4K datasets and in-the-wild images demonstrate that our method outperforms state-of-the-art autoregressive and two-stage baselines, effectively circumventing the inherent limitations of sequential mesh modeling. A blind user study from 3D practitioners confirms strong perceptual preference for our results.

</details>


### [47] [GHR-VLM: Making Zero-Shot Transit Video Analytics Realizable with Grounded Hybrid Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13569)
*Kaicong Huang,Weiheng Oh,Ruimin Ke*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GHR-VLM is a zero-shot framework for transit-bus video analytics that combines lightweight edge-based monitoring (tracking door status, segmenting passenger clips) with a backend VLM that performs coarse-to-fine spatiotemporal reasoning to identify boarding passengers and classify payment behavior, reducing cloud inference costs while avoiding the need for payment-specific training data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Supervised video models require task-specific annotations, while directly applying VLMs to long onboard videos is unreliable and costly. The paper aims to leverage the complementary strengths of both approaches for transit video understanding.

Method: Proposes an edge-cloud hybrid framework: (1) lightweight edge-based monitor tracks door status and segments passenger clips, (2) backend VLM identifies boarding passengers and classifies payment behavior via two-stage coarse-to-fine refinement of spatiotemporal evidence using grounded passenger clips and contact sheets.

Result: Evaluated on 486 minutes of real-world bus surveillance video, demonstrating potential for passenger-level payment analytics while revealing challenges posed by degraded video conditions.

Conclusion: Grounded edge-cloud reasoning can enable zero-shot passenger-level payment analytics without payment-specific training data, reducing cloud inference costs, though degraded video quality remains a challenge.

Abstract: Transit video understanding can provide valuable fine-grained data that conventional passenger counters and fare systems cannot capture. However, supervised video models require task-specific annotations, while applying vision-language models (VLMs) directly to long onboard videos is unreliable and costly. To leverage the complementary strengths of both approaches, we propose GHR-VLM, a visual grounded hybrid reasoning framework for zero-shot transit-bus video analytics. It is motivated by the observation that explicit visual grounding can improve VLM reasoning by converting long surveillance streams into compact, passenger-centered spatiotemporal evidence. Specifically, we propose an edge-cloud design in which a lightweight edge-based monitor continuously tracks door status and segments passenger clips. A backend VLM then identifies boarding passengers and classifies payment behavior through a two-stage coarse-to-fine refinement of spatiotemporal evidence. By invoking the VLM only on grounded passenger clips and contact sheets, GHR-VLM reduces cloud inference, avoids payment-specific training data, and supplies the localized evidence that VLMs otherwise struggle to identify. Evaluation on 486 minutes of real-world bus surveillance video demonstrates the potential of grounded edge-cloud reasoning for passenger-level payment analytics while highlighting the challenges posed by degraded video conditions.

</details>


### [48] [UniPhysGen: Unified Physical Grounding for Simulation-Ready 3D Assets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13586)
*Xian Li,Rong Wei,Lujie Yang,Haolin Huang,Junyuan Fang,Siliang Tang,Jun Xiao,Rui Tang,Juncheng Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniPhys is a scalable framework that transforms raw 3D assets into simulation-ready assets with unified physical semantics (articulation + intrinsic properties). It produces UniPhys-40K dataset and UniPhys-Bench benchmark, plus a UniPhysGen model achieving SOTA on articulation grounding and physical property estimation for embodied AI/robotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing 3D assets lack unified physical semantics (articulation and intrinsic properties) needed for realistic robotic interaction; current methods treat these semantics independently or rely on canonicalized structures, limiting robustness across heterogeneous assets.

Method: UniPhys is a scalable automatic framework for transforming raw 3D assets into simulation-ready ones. UniPhysGen is a unified physical grounding model that jointly reasons over articulation semantics and intrinsic physical properties, incorporating geometry-robust articulation grounding to mitigate geometric shortcut bias under heterogeneous part decompositions.

Result: State-of-the-art performance across articulation grounding and intrinsic physical property estimation tasks; resulting assets are directly deployable in robotic simulation environments for realistic physical interaction.

Conclusion: UniPhys demonstrates that unified physical semantics can be automatically and robustly extracted from heterogeneous 3D assets, enabling direct deployment in embodied AI and robotic simulations.

Abstract: Physically grounded 3D assets are increasingly important for embodied AI and robotic simulation. However, most existing 3D assets lack unified physical semantics, including articulation semantics and intrinsic physical properties, required for realistic interaction. Current approaches either treat these semantics independently or rely on canonicalized object structures, limiting robustness across heterogeneous 3D assets. We present UniPhys, a scalable framework for automatically transforming raw 3D assets into simulation-ready assets with unified physical semantics. Based on UniPhys, we construct UniPhys-40K, a large-scale physically grounded dataset, together with UniPhys-Bench, a carefully verified benchmark for unified physical grounding evaluation. We further introduce UniPhysGen, a unified physical grounding model that jointly reasons over articulation semantics and intrinsic physical properties. UniPhysGen incorporates geometry-robust articulation grounding to mitigate geometric shortcut bias under heterogeneous part decompositions. Extensive experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across articulation grounding and intrinsic physical property estimation tasks, while the resulting assets can be directly deployed in robotic simulation environments for realistic physical interaction. Our code and dataset will be available at https://github.com/breezexian/UniPhysGen.

</details>


### [49] [OvisOCR2 Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13639)
*Shiyin Lu,Yinglun Li,Yu Xia,Yuhui Chen,An-Yang Ji,Jun-Peng Jiang,Qing-Guo Chen,Jianshan Zhao,En Lin,Haijun Li,Cheng Qin,Zhao Xu,Weihua Luo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OvisOCR2 is a 0.8B end-to-end document parsing model that converts page images to Markdown, achieving state-of-the-art scores of 96.58 on OmniDocBench v1.6 and 75.06 on PureDocBench through a training pipeline involving SFT, RL with multi-component rewards, on-policy distillation, and model fusion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate that end-to-end document parsing models can outperform traditional pipeline methods on public benchmarks, and to provide a lightweight (0.8B parameters) yet high-performing solution for document understanding.

Method: The model uses a data engine combining filtered real-document annotations with synthetic pages (HTML-derived rendering and Markdown targets). Training involves supervised fine-tuning, reinforcement learning on a 4B branch with multi-component reward design, on-policy distillation into the 0.8B model, and model fusion.

Result: Achieves SOTA overall score of 96.58 on OmniDocBench v1.6 (first end-to-end model to top the leaderboard), highest Avg3 score of 75.06 on PureDocBench, and best overall performance on an in-house benchmark covering long-tail and challenging scenarios.

Conclusion: OvisOCR2 highlights the potential of end-to-end document parsing over pipeline methods and demonstrates strong generalization and robustness across diverse scenarios.

Abstract: We introduce OvisOCR2, a 0.8B document parsing model. OvisOCR2 is designed as an end-to-end parser: given a document page image, it generates a Markdown representation in natural reading order, covering text, formulas, tables, and visual regions. We build a data engine that combines filtered real-document annotations with synthetic pages whose rendered images and Markdown targets are derived from the same HTML source. The training recipe includes supervised fine-tuning, reinforcement learning on a 4B branch with a multi-component reward design, on-policy distillation into the 0.8B model, and model fusion. On OmniDocBench v1.6, OvisOCR2 achieves a state-of-the-art overall score of 96.58, placing an end-to-end model at the top of this leaderboard previously dominated by pipeline methods and highlighting the potential of end-to-end document parsing. On PureDocBench, OvisOCR2 also achieves the highest Avg3 score of 75.06. Beyond these two public benchmarks, we evaluate OvisOCR2 on an in-house benchmark designed to cover a broader set of long-tail and challenging scenarios. OvisOCR2 obtains the best overall performance among the compared methods, providing further evidence of its generalization and robustness. OvisOCR2 is available at https://huggingface.co/ATH-MaaS/OvisOCR2.

</details>


### [50] [Human4K: A Large-Scale 4K Multi-View Mocap Dataset for Whole-Body 3D Human Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13646)
*Tianshun Han,Ziyu Shi,Lijian Liu,Ajian Liu,Benjia Zhou,Hugo Jair Escalante,Yanyan Liang,Sergio Escalera,Zhen Lei,Jun Wan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Human4K is a large-scale 4K multi-view whole-body human reconstruction dataset with mocap-accurate SMPL-X annotations, containing over six million 8-view 4K images of 11 subjects performing complex motions, which significantly improves whole-body reconstruction especially for hands, feet and depth-ambiguous limbs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing datasets lack high-resolution images, high-precision annotations and diverse whole-body motions, causing current 3D human reconstruction models to produce unstable geometry, inaccurate limb articulation and unreliable predictions under depth ambiguity or self-occlusion.

Method: The authors built an eight-view high-resolution camera system synchronized with a Vicon motion capture setup to capture over six million 4K images from 11 subjects performing complex, highly articulated and strongly self-occluded full-body motions. All sequences are processed by a Motion-Retargeting and Refinement Module (MRRM) to ensure precise alignment for the full body and extremities.

Result: Training with Human4K consistently improves whole-body reconstruction on standard benchmarks, with particularly large gains for hands, feet and depth-ambiguous limb configurations.

Conclusion: The proposed Human4K dataset effectively addresses the limitations of existing datasets and enables more robust 3D human reconstruction in challenging real-world scenarios, especially for extremities and depth-ambiguous poses.

Abstract: Recent advances in 3D human reconstruction have improved overall performance, yet current models still fail in the most challenging real-world scenarios. They often produce unstable geometry, inaccurate limb articulation and unreliable predictions under depth ambiguity or self-occlusion. A key reason is that existing datasets still lack the combination of high-resolution images, high-precision annotations and diverse whole-body motions required to support robust reconstruction. To address this gap, we present Human4K, a large-scale 4K multi-view whole-body human reconstruction dataset with mocap-accurate SMPL-X annotations. Human4K contains over six million 4K images captured by an eight-view high-resolution camera system synchronized with a professional Vicon motion capture setup, covering 11 subjects performing complex, highly articulated and strongly self-occluded full-body motions. All sequences are processed by a Motion-Retargeting and Refinement Module (MRRM) to ensure precise alignment for the full body and extremities. Experimental results show that training with Human4K consistently improves whole-body reconstruction on standard benchmarks, with particularly large gains for hands, feet and depth-ambiguous limb configurations.

</details>


### [51] [Beyond Color Geometry: Evaluating Human-Like Color Representations in Vision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13647)
*Ayan Igali,Pakizar Shamoi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper evaluates how well Vision Transformers (ViTs) represent color in a human-like manner, using a fuzzy perceptual model with 86 graded categories from human survey data. The framework measures category boundaries, compactness, and graded alignment beyond color geometry. Masked Autoencoders (MAE) achieve the best beyond-geometry alignment, with layer-wise analysis showing MAE preserves color structure toward output, while language-supervised models encode color more in relation to foreground objects.
