The results show human-like color grounding is multi-faceted and should not be reduced to a single metric.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing evaluations of color representations rely on geometric spaces (e.g., CIELAB) or discrete color labels, which capture perceptual distance or category membership but fail to capture the graded way humans organize colors.

Method: The authors propose a framework using a fuzzy perceptual model with 86 graded categories fitted to human survey data. The framework can be applied to any image encoder and measures three properties: category boundaries, category compactness, and graded alignment beyond what color geometry alone can explain.

Result: Across eleven Vision Transformer encoders, category-level results are broadly similar, but graded alignment differs substantially. Masked Autoencoders achieve the strongest beyond-geometry alignment with non-overlapping confidence intervals. Layer-wise analysis shows MAE preserves color structure toward the output. On natural images, MAE represents surface color globally while language-supervised models encode color more relative to foreground objects.

Conclusion: Human-like color grounding has several distinct aspects that should not be reduced to a single score. Different vision encoders exhibit qualitatively different color representations, particularly in graded alignment and how color relates to object versus surface information.

Abstract: Do vision models see colors the way humans do? Existing evaluations of color representations usually compare them with geometric spaces such as CIELAB or with discrete color labels. These references capture perceptual distance or category membership, but not the graded way in which people organize colors. We evaluate color grounding against a fuzzy perceptual model with 86 graded categories fitted to human survey data. The framework can be applied to any image encoder and measures three complementary properties: category boundaries, category compactness, and graded alignment beyond what color geometry alone can explain. Across eleven Vision Transformer encoders, the category-level results are broadly similar, whereas graded alignment differs substantially. Masked Autoencoders achieve the strongest beyond-geometry alignment, with confidence intervals that do not overlap those of the other encoders. A layer-wise analysis further shows that masked reconstruction preserves this structure toward the output. On natural images, MAE represents surface color globally, while language-supervised models encode color more strongly in relation to the foreground object. These results show that human-like color grounding has several distinct aspects that should not be reduced to a single score.

</details>


### [52] [From Surface Forecasting to Observability Forecasting: A Latent World Model for Cloud-Aware EO Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13651)
*Mohanad Albughdadi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LeWorldModel, a joint-embedding predictive world model adapted for cloud-aware Earth Observation, outperforms persistence and competes with LightGBM on observability forecasting (predicting surface visibility and when a usable view returns) using EarthNet2021 data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The bottleneck in Earth Observation processing is not image arrival but surface visibility (cloud cover). The authors study this as an observability forecasting problem to predict whether the next acquisition will be usable and when a usable view will return.

Method: Adapt LeWorldModel (joint-embedding predictive architecture world model) to cloud-aware Earth Observation sequences. Pipeline converts raw minicubes into episodic HDF5 sequences with 5 channels (blue, green, red, NIR, cloud mask) and 8 meteorological/calendar covariates. Model has 18.0M trainable parameters, trained from scratch on 23,904 episodes. Evaluated under locked protocol with linear probes.

Result: LeWorldModel consistently outperforms persistence on observability (balanced accuracy 0.769-0.887 vs 0.493-0.556 for next-step; 0.602-0.806 vs 0.120-0.369 for first-usable-horizon). Against LightGBM, LeWM is better on continuous clear/cloud regression and exact recovery timing on valsplit, IID, extreme; LightGBM is stronger on binary any-usable-within-six task and more robust on OOD. LeWM also produces strong anomaly signals under synthetic temporal inconsistencies.

Conclusion: LeWorldModel is effective for observability forecasting in Earth Observation, significantly outperforming persistence and showing competitive performance against LightGBM across multiple evaluation splits.

Abstract: The bottleneck of Earth Observation processing chains is not the arrival of new imagery but whether the surface is actually visible when the image arrives. We study this as an observability forecasting problem on EarthNet2021. Given recent multispectral imagery and exogenous weather drivers, the goal is to predict whether the next acquisition will be usable and, if not, when a usable view is likely to return. To do this, we adapt LeWorldModel, a joint-embedding predictive architecture world model, to cloud-aware Earth Observation sequences. The final pipeline converts raw minicubes into episodic HDF5 sequences with five image channels (blue, green, red, near-infrared, cloud mask) and eight meteorological and calendar covariates. The resulting model has 18.0M trainable parameters and is trained from scratch on 23,904 training episodes. The trained leWorldModel is evaluated under a locked protocol: linear probes are fit on train only, calibration choices are set on an internal validation split, and the fitted heads are then frozen for valsplit, IID, OOD, and extreme evaluation. On the full frozen-bundle observability benchmark, LeWorldModel consistently outperforms persistence. For next-step usability, balanced accuracy ranges from 0.769 to 0.887, compared with 0.493 to 0.556 for persistence. For exact first-usable-horizon prediction, accuracy ranges from 0.602 to 0.806, compared with 0.120 to 0.369 for persistence. Against a frozen LightGBM baseline fit on the same training windows, LeWorldModel is better on continuous clear/cloud regression and on exact recovery timing on valsplit, IID, and extreme, while LightGBM is stronger on the simpler binary any-usable-within-six task and is more robust on OOD. In separate sampled diagnostic analyses, LeWM also produces strong ranking-based anomaly signals under synthetic temporal inconsistencies.

</details>


### [53] [Exploratory, Communicative, and Deployable: Vision-Driven Embodied Agents for Open-World Mobile Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13653)
*Boyu Mi,Mengchen Ma,Yifei Yao,Xing Gao,Junting Chen,Yangzi Li,Zihou Zhu,Guohao Li,Zhenfei Yin,Tai Wang,Yao Mu,Jiangmiao Pang,Hanqing Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: REAL is a sim-to-real-consistent agentic framework for open-world mobile manipulation that integrates simulated users, hierarchical training (supervised fine-tuning + online RL), and a benchmark (REAL-Bench) of 241 tasks; it achieves 56.9% on interactive tasks and 78.3% real-world success rate on a dual-arm mobile robot.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing frameworks for embodied agent deployment rely on privileged simulator states or assume complete instructions, failing to address realistic challenges like active exploration, visual grounding, and interactive intent disambiguation in open-world settings.

Method: REAL establishes sim-to-real-consistent environment APIs without oracle perception and integrates a simulated user for human-in-the-loop interaction. It designs diverse task compositions for data collection, supervised fine-tuning, and online reinforcement learning with a hierarchical training pipeline to optimize agent performance.

Result: The agent outperforms leading commercial closed-source VLMs on interactive tasks with a 56.9% success rate. On a physical dual-arm mobile robot, it achieves 78.3% end-to-end success rate over 60 real-world episodes, demonstrating robust zero-shot transferability.

Conclusion: The paper introduces REAL, an agentic framework for open-world mobile manipulation that bridges the sim-to-real gap without oracle perception. Their trained agent achieves 56.9% success rate on interactive tasks, outperforming commercial VLMs, and demonstrates robust zero-shot transferability with 78.3% end-to-end success rate on a real dual-arm mobile robot over 60 episodes, validating that sim-to-real-consistent design effectively enables long-horizon mobile manipulation in unseen household scenarios.

Abstract: Real-world deployment of embodied agents requires active exploration, visual grounding, and interactive intent disambiguation. However, existing frameworks often rely on privileged simulator states or assume complete instructions, bypassing realistic deployment challenges. To bridge this gap, we present REAL, an agentic framework for open-world mobile manipulation. REAL establishes sim-to-real-consistent environment APIs without oracle perception and integrates a simulated user to enable human-in-the-loop interaction. Within this environment, we design diverse task compositions to drive data collection, supervised fine-tuning, and online reinforcement learning, systematically optimizing agent performance. To comprehensively evaluate this approach, we introduce REAL-Bench, a benchmark spanning 241 tasks across active exploration, visual distraction, articulated manipulation, and interactive disambiguation.
  Experimental results demonstrate that our trained agent outperforms leading commercial closed-source VLMs on interactive tasks with a 56.9% success rate. Further empirical analysis reveals that our hierarchical training pipeline successfully aligns the model's tool-use capabilities while maintaining robust open-vocabulary reasoning under extended exploration horizons. Finally, we deploy and evaluate our framework on a physical dual-arm mobile robot, where it achieves a 78.3% end-to-end success rate over 60 real-world episodes. These physical trials demonstrate robust zero-shot transferability to unseen household scenarios, validating that our sim-to-real-consistent design successfully bridges the reality gap for long-horizon mobile manipulation. Code is available at https://github.com/InternRobotics/REAL.

</details>


### [54] [T3HG-Editor: Text-driven 3D Human Garment Editing with Body Priors Embedded in SMPL-X](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13654)
*Shaoru Sun,Xingtao Wang,Zihan Ma,Wenrui Li,Jiantao Zhou,Debin Zhao,Xiaopeng Fan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: T3HG-Editor is a text-driven 3D human garment editing method that leverages SMPL-X priors to achieve high-fidelity, garment-consistent editing through three stages: editable Gaussian obtainment, garment consistent editing, and Gaussian updating with overflow pruning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Text-driven 3D human garment editing remains underexplored, and existing 3DGE methods suffer from low fidelity, distortions, and garment inconsistencies when applied to human garments. The authors identify SMPL-X as a rich prior for virtual humans that could address these issues.

Method: T3HG-Editor operates in three stages: (1) Obtainment of editable Gaussians - seeding Gaussians along SMPL-X normals and using 2D mask constraints to localize target Gaussians; (2) Garment consistent editing - aggregating tokens across multiple views for the same SMPL-X vertex and propagating back to enforce consistency without extra training; (3) Gaussian updating with overflow pruning - using an SDF defined on SMPL-X combined with 2D semantic masks to prune overflowing Gaussians and prevent contamination.

Result: Experiments on multiple subjects and diverse garment types show T3HG-Editor outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both editing quality and garment consistency.

Conclusion: Leveraging SMPL-X geometry and joint priors enables high-fidelity, garment-consistent text-driven 3D human garment editing, overcoming limitations of previous 3DGE approaches.

Abstract: While 3D Gaussian Editing (3DGE) has seen substantial progress, text-driven 3D human garment editing remains largely underexplored. Existing 3DGE works typically follow a paradigm that applies 2D editing techniques to multi-view rendered images and updates 3D Gaussians based on the modified images. Extending such methods to 3D human garment editing suffers from low-fidelity outcomes, caused by introduced distortions and garment inconsistencies. A promising breakthrough opportunity arises from the SMPL eXpressive (SMPL-X) model that embodies rich prior information for virtual humans. Motivated by this insight, we propose a text-driven 3D human garment editor termed T3HG-Editor, which delivers high-fidelity and garment consistent results by leveraging geometry and joint priors embedded in SMPL-X. Specifically, T3HG-Editor contains three stages, namely obtainment of editable Gaussians, garment consistent editing, and Gaussian updating with overflow pruning. The obtainment of editable Gaussians begins with seeding Gaussians along SMPL-X normals to generate sufficient near surface Gaussians, followed by a 2D mask constraint that precisely localizes the target Gaussians to be edited. The garment consistent editing aggregates tokens corresponding to the same SMPL-X vertex across multiple views and propagates them to their original views, enforcing garment consistency without requiring additional training. Gaussian updating with overflow pruning employs a Signed Distance Function (SDF) defined on SMPL-X to construct a human distance field, which is then integrated with a 2D semantic mask to prune overflowing Gaussians, thus preventing contamination of non-target regions. Experiments on multiple subjects and diverse garment types demonstrate that T3HG-Editor outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both editing quality and garment consistency.

</details>


### [55] [FreeLit: Paired-Free Indoor Relighting via Physics-Guided Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13656)
*Chi-En Yen,Duy-Khanh Ngo,Wen-Wei Tang,Huu-Phu Do,Wen-Hsiao Peng,Ching-Chun Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FreeLit is a paired-free framework for controllable indoor scene relighting that uses a physics-guided illumination prior and relighting-guided intrinsic stabilization to manipulate light-source location, color, and intensity via diffusion-based synthesis, achieving stable and physically consistent results without requiring costly paired multi-illumination datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing data-driven methods require costly paired multi-illumination datasets that fail to scale for accurate light-source-level control, while inverse-rendering methods lack robustness in challenging conditions (e.g., low-light scenes).

Method: FreeLit constructs a physics-guided illumination prior from intrinsic scene properties to generate a structured lightmap and pseudo-relit image, which guide diffusion-based synthesis. It also introduces a relighting-guided intrinsic stabilization strategy using structure-aware distillation and consistency constraints to enforce illumination-invariant reflectance, plus controllability-oriented evaluation metrics.

Result: FreeLit achieves stable, physically consistent, and controllable relighting with improved robustness in low-light indoor scenes, without requiring paired supervision.

Conclusion: FreeLit demonstrates that controllable indoor relighting can be achieved without paired multi-illumination data, leveraging physics-guided priors and intrinsic stabilization for robustness and explicit light-source-level control.

Abstract: Image-based indoor scene relighting remains challenging due to the complex interplay between cluttered geometry and local illumination, requiring precise modeling of light position, color, and intensity. Existing data-driven methods implicitly learn this relationship via paired multi-illumination datasets. Nevertheless, this data is costly and fails to scale, which is essential for accurate light-source-level control. Conversely, inverse-rendering methods reduce the data dependency by incorporating physical priors; however, they lack the robustness of intrinsic estimation in challenging conditions.
  In this paper, we present FreeLit, a paired-free framework for controllable indoor relighting that explicitly manipulates light-source location, color, and intensity. Instead of relying on paired supervision, we construct a physics-guided illumination prior from intrinsic scene properties, generating a structured lightmap along with a pseudo-relit image to guide diffusion-based synthesis. To address instability in intrinsic estimation, especially in low-light scenes, we introduce a relighting-guided intrinsic stabilization strategy that enforces illumination-invariant reflectance through structure-aware distillation and consistency constraints. Furthermore, we propose controllability-oriented evaluation metrics to quantify alignment with user-specified illumination color and intensity. Experimental results demonstrate that FreeLit achieves stable, physically consistent, and controllable relighting, with improved robustness in low-light indoor scenes, without requiring paired supervision.

</details>


### [56] [Fine-grained CLIP fine-tuning with self-annotated region alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13661)
*Chenyang Zhao,Wei Lin,Antoni B. Chan,Janet H. Hsiao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SFF-CLIP enhances CLIP's fine-grained dense feature representation through a fine-tuning method that only requires image-text pairs (no additional region annotations), using a run-time region-phrase alignment scheme with text-specific heat maps, while preserving CLIP's original global visual-semantic consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: CLIP has limitations in fine-grained dense feature representation because its pre-training focuses on whole image-text matching. Existing fine-tuning approaches require additional region annotations (which limit semantic diversity and increase data processing effort) and often degrade CLIP's global visual representation ability.

Method: SFF-CLIP proposes a run-time region-phrase alignment scheme that extracts concept phrases from input sentences and aligns them with corresponding region-based features using text-specific heat maps, requiring only image-text pairs as input.

Result: SFF-CLIP achieves significant performance improvements on fine-grained dense feature representation tasks while maintaining the original CLIP's performance on image-level tasks.

Conclusion: The proposed method effectively bypasses the limitations of existing CLIP fine-tuning approaches, enhancing fine-grained abilities without additional annotations or sacrificing global representation quality.

Abstract: Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training (CLIP) has been shown to have limitations in its fine-grained dense feature representation, due to its pre-training focusing on matching the whole image to a text description. Considering the large data and computational burden in pre-training a vision-language model from scratch, a series of works aim to enhance the fine-grained ability of CLIP through a fine-tuning scheme. However, existing works suffer from a variety of limitations: additional region annotations are usually required, which limits the semantic diversity due to the predefined categories and leads to a large effort to process the training data; and they usually sacrifice CLIP's original ability for global visual representation. To bypass these limitations, we propose SFF-CLIP (Self-annotated Fine-grained Fine-tuning for CLIP), which only uses image-text pairs as input to boost the fine-grained representation ability in the CLIP fine-tuning, while maintaining the global visual-semantic consistency. Concretely, a run-time region-phrase alignment scheme is designed, which obtains concept phrases from the input sentence, and aligns them with corresponding extracted region-based features using text-specific heat maps. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SFF-CLIP leads to significant performance improvements on fine-grained dense feature representation, as well as maintaining the performance of the original CLIP on image-level tasks. Code will be released later.

</details>


### [57] [Learning Speaker Identity Beyond Language and Modality Constraints: Insights from the POLY-SIM 2026 Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13669)
*Marta Moscati,Muhammad Saad Saeed,Marina Zanoni,Mubashir Noman,Rohan Kumar Das,Monorama Swain,Yassin Terraf,Yufang Hou,Elisabeth Andre,Khalid Mahmood Malik,Markus Schedl,Shah Nawaz*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The POLY-SIM 2026 challenge addresses real-world multimodal speaker identification where audio-visual modalities may be incomplete and speakers may be multilingual, providing a standardized evaluation benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world multimodal speaker identification faces two critical challenges that existing systems ignore: (1) missing modalities due to occlusions, hardware failures, or privacy constraints; and (2) multilingual speakers introducing linguistic variability. These issues severely degrade robustness and generalization.

Method: The POLY-SIM 2026 challenge establishes a standardized evaluation framework to benchmark solutions that handle incomplete audio-visual modalities and multilingual speakers in speaker identification tasks.

Result: No experimental results are presented; this is a challenge proposal paper describing the problem setup and evaluation framework for the POLY-SIM 2026 benchmark.

Conclusion: The POLY-SIM 2026 challenge aims to drive progress in robust multimodal speaker identification by addressing modality incompleteness and multilingual variability through a standardized comparison platform.

Abstract: Multimodal speaker identification systems typically assume the availability of complete and homogeneous audio-visual modalities during both training and testing, and assume each speaker only speaks a single language. However, in real-world applications, such assumptions often do not hold. Visual or audio information may be missing due to occlusions, camera or microphone failures, or privacy constraints. Multilingual speakers introduce additional complexity due to linguistic variability across languages. These situations constitute substantial challenges for the robustness and generalization capabilities of multimodal speaker identification systems. Aim of the POLY-SIM 2026 challenge is to address these aspects of speaker identification and to provide a standardized setup for the comparison of the proposed solutions.

</details>


### [58] [WAVE-Stereo: Warp-Aligned Volume Encoding for Stereo Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13674)
*Zehan Liu,Yage He,Xianwu Gong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: WAVE-Stereo proposes a unified framework combining correlation volumes and feature warping for iterative stereo matching, achieving competitive zero-shot generalization on five benchmarks with real-time inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing iterative stereo methods model explicit matching search via correlation volumes and local residual refinement via warped features separately, lacking integration of these complementary cues.

Method: (1) GeoWarp Correspondence Encoder (GWCE) encodes matching search, residual alignment, and disparity prior in parallel at ConvGRU input; (2) Periodic Global Context Propagation (PGCP) mitigates degradation in textureless regions by propagating global spatial information periodically.

Result: 3.18% D1-all on KITTI 2015, 4.42% Bad-2.0 on Booster, 66ms real-time inference, competitive zero-shot generalization on five benchmarks (Middlebury, ETH3D, KITTI 2012/2015, Booster).

Conclusion: WAVE-Stereo achieves a favorable accuracy-efficiency balance, demonstrating that jointly modeling correlation volumes and feature warping yields strong generalization without external foundation models.

Abstract: Existing iterative stereo matching methods primarily adopt two types of correspondence representation: explicit matching search via correlation volumes and local residual refinement via warped features, yet the two remain separately modeled. We propose WAVE-Stereo, built on a core insight: correlation volumes and feature warping provide complementary matching cues. \textbf{GeoWarp Correspondence Encoder (GWCE)} encodes matching search, residual alignment, and disparity prior in parallel at the ConvGRU input. To mitigate matching degradation in textureless regions, we propose \textbf{Periodic Global Context Propagation (PGCP)}, which propagates global spatial information in a periodic manner. On five real-world benchmarks -- Middlebury, ETH3D, KITTI 2012, KITTI 2015, and Booster -- WAVE-Stereo achieves competitive zero-shot generalization accuracy without any external foundation model prior, achieving 3.18\% D1-all on KITTI 2015, 4.42\% Bad-2.0 on Booster, and 66ms real-time inference, striking a favorable balance between accuracy and efficiency. Our code is available at https://github.com/yamanoko-do/WAVE-Stereo.

</details>


### [59] [Towards Spatial Supersensing in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13681)
*Tianjun Gu,Tianyu Xin,Kuan Zhang,Bowen Yang,Kok-Chung Chua,Peize Li,Xinran Zhang,Yupeng Chen,Qiyue Zhao,Qinlei Xie,Jianhang Liu,Yucheng Lu,Yinan Han,Marco Pavone,Yiming Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VSI-Super-Wild is a large-scale benchmark for evaluating spatial supersensing over long temporal horizons in diverse real-world scenes, containing 6,980 human-verified QA pairs from 442 real-world videos (up to 4+ hours). It reveals that models consistently fail at coherent world-state tracking over time, with four diagnosed failure modes: spatial collapse, semantic shortcuts, insufficient update, and instance confusion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing spatial supersensing benchmarks rely on synthetic long videos (concatenated random clips) mostly limited to household scenes, lacking real-world continuity and diversity. The authors aim to address this gap by introducing a benchmark that probes real-world temporal continuity and diverse in-the-wild scenes.

Method: The benchmark systematically probes three components of world state (agent, objects, environment) using 6,980 human-verified QA pairs from 442 real-world videos across 8 scene categories, including long-form recordings exceeding 4 hours. Models are evaluated on coherent world-state tracking over time.

Result: Models consistently fail at tasks requiring coherent world-state tracking over time despite advances in static image understanding. Performance degrades with world-state complexity and temporal horizon. Four failure modes are identified: spatial collapse, semantic shortcuts, insufficient update, and instance confusion.

Conclusion: Current models lack mechanisms to bind objects, agents, and environments into a unified spatial world model, revealing a fundamental gap that defines the path forward for spatial supersensing research.

Abstract: Humans can efficiently parse continuous sensory streams, from hours to years, scaffolding an internal world model that grounds spatial reasoning and prediction. To mimic this capacity, spatial supersensing challenges multimodal models to move beyond linguistic understanding toward true world modeling. However, their benchmark relies on synthetic long videos, formed by concatenating random short clips, and is mostly limited to household scenes, leaving real-world continuity and diversity underexplored. To address the gap, we introduce $\textbf{VSI-Super-Wild}$, a large-scale benchmark for evaluating spatial supersensing over long temporal horizons in diverse in-the-wild scenes. Notably, inspired by cognitive studies on how humans structure experience, we systematically probe the full triad of world state: the agent (observer), objects (scene items), and the environment (places and global layout). In total, VSI-Super-Wild contains $\textbf{6,980}$ human-verified question-answer pairs derived from $\textbf{442}$ real-world videos spanning 8 scene categories, including long-form recordings exceeding 4 hours. Results on VSI-Super-Wild expose a fundamental disconnect: despite advances in static image understanding, models consistently fail at tasks that require coherent world-state tracking over time. We characterize how performance degrades with world-state complexity and temporal horizon, and diagnose four failure modes: spatial collapse, semantic shortcuts, insufficient update, and instance confusion. This taxonomy reveals that models lack mechanisms to bind objects, agents, and environments into a unified spatial world model, a fundamental gap that defines the path forward for spatial supersensing.

</details>


### [60] [Calibrated Closed-Form Uncertainty for Radiative Gaussian Splatting in Sparse-View CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13682)
*Chulin Zhao,Yiran Xu,Shu Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Radiative Gaussian splatting is extended with a variational density posterior that yields closed-form, exact predictive variance in both volume and projection space, enabling calibrated uncertainty quantification for sparse-view CT. The method ranks true error on 14/15 benchmark scenes across view budgets, outperforming perturbation ensembles. A three-regime analysis of acquisition scores motivates a coverage-gated acquisition policy, with dose-adaptive stopping left for future work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing radiative Gaussian splatting for sparse-view CT reconstruction outputs point estimates without trustworthiness information. The paper aims to equip it with principled, calibrated uncertainty quantification that propagates in closed form.

Method: Exploits the linearity of transmissive X-ray imaging (projection and voxelization are linear in per-Gaussian densities) to equip radiative Gaussians with a variational density posterior. Predictive variance propagates in closed form in both volume space (σ²(x)=∑ g_i(x)² s_i²) and projection space (Var[I_p]=∑ w_{i,p}² s_i²). Calibration is assessed via Spearman / AUSE / ECE with temperature scaling.

Result: The method ranks true reconstruction error on 14/15 scenes across three view budgets; 9/15 meet magnitude-calibration target after temperature scaling. Perturbation-ensemble heuristic fails (rank correlation as low as -0.08). Identifies three acquisition regimes (flat, pathological, anisotropic) where principled uncertainty earns a measurable premium only in the anisotropic regime, motivating a coverage-gated, maturity-scheduled acquisition policy.

Conclusion: Principled calibrated uncertainty outperforms heuristics like perturbation ensembles for Gaussian-splatting CT. A coverage-gated acquisition policy is motivated by the three-regime analysis. A dose-adaptive stopping rule is suggested but left for future experimental validation.

Abstract: Radiative Gaussian splatting has made sparse-view CT reconstruction fast, but existing methods output point estimates with no notion of where the reconstruction can be trusted. We exploit a property of transmissive X-ray imaging that RGB splatting cannot claim -- projection and voxelization are strictly linear in the per-Gaussian densities -- to equip radiative Gaussians with a variational density posterior whose predictive variance propagates in closed form, exactly, in a single forward pass, in both volume space ($σ^2(x)=\sum_i g_i(x)^2 s_i^2$) and projection space ($\mathrm{Var}[I_p]=\sum_i w_{i,p}^2 s_i^2$). We present the first systematic calibration study for Gaussian-splatting CT (Spearman / AUSE / ECE with temperature scaling), showing that the resulting per-voxel uncertainty ranks true reconstruction error on 14 of 15 scenes of the official benchmark across three view budgets -- 9 of 15 additionally meeting our magnitude-calibration target after a single temperature -- while the perturbation-ensemble heuristic of concurrent work, transplanted to voxel space under the same protocol on our development scenes, does not (rank correlation as low as $-0.08$). We then dissect why uncalibrated acquisition scores can nevertheless select acceptable views, identifying three regimes -- flat (isotropic, balanced), pathological (degenerate coverage), and anisotropic -- and showing, in controlled single-scene testbeds, that principled uncertainty earns a measurable premium only in the last, motivating a coverage-gated, maturity-scheduled acquisition policy; the same calibrated posterior further points toward a dose-adaptive stopping rule, whose experimental validation we leave to future work.

</details>


### [61] [DNA: Dual-stage Native Attribution for Generated Image Source Tracing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13685)
*Chao Wang,Kejiang Chen,Zijin Yang,Yaofei Wang,Yuang Qi,Weiming Zhang,Nenghai Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DNA is a training-free, coarse-to-fine framework for attributing AI-generated images to their source models, leveraging stratified attribution signals (VAE-level for family, backbone-level for variants) via Autoencoder Double-Reconstruction and Native Prediction Consistency, achieving 89.11% accuracy on a 24-model benchmark (DNA-30K) with random guessing below 1%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing image attribution methods struggle with within-family variants (highly similar models) due to reliance on watermarking (degrades quality), large-scale supervised training (limited generalization), or single-signal reconstruction (poor discrimination). The gap is an effective, training-free approach for open-set attribution across both family-level and variant-level sources.

Method: Proposes Dual-stage Native Attribution (DNA): (1) Coarse-grained stage uses Autoencoder Double-Reconstruction (AEDR) for open-set family-level screening. (2) Fine-grained stage uses Native Prediction Consistency (NPC) comparing native prediction errors of within-family variants across multiple noise levels under semantic conditioning, with normalized calibrated scores for closed-set model-level attribution. No additional neural-network training is required.

Result: On the DNA-30K benchmark (30K images, 24 candidate models, 6 families: denoising diffusion + flow matching), DNA achieves 89.11% end-to-end attribution accuracy. It outperforms the strongest baseline by 33.81% even when AEDR alone is used as the coarse-grained stage. Random guessing accuracy is below 1%.

Conclusion: Stratified attribution signals in latent generative models (VAE vs. backbone) can be exploited without training. The coarse-to-fine DNA framework effectively handles open-set family screening and closed-set within-family variant discrimination, significantly surpassing existing methods on challenging within-family attribution tasks.

Abstract: The rapid evolution of image generation has produced numerous within-family variants, making source-model attribution of suspect images increasingly important for digital forensics. Existing proactive methods rely on watermark embedding or model modification, which may degrade visual quality and limit deployment flexibility. Passive methods often rely on large-scale supervised training or a single reconstruction signal, limiting their ability to handle unknown sources and distinguish highly similar within-family variants. We observe that attribution signals in latent generative models are naturally stratified across architectural levels: VAE-level cues reflect family-shared information, whereas backbone-level cues capture variant-specific behaviors. Motivated by this insight, we propose Dual-stage Native Attribution (DNA), a coarse-to-fine framework that follows this hierarchy without additional neural-network training. The coarse-grained stage uses Autoencoder Double-Reconstruction (AEDR) for efficient open-set family-level screening. The fine-grained stage performs closed-set model-level attribution with Native Prediction Consistency (NPC), which compares native prediction errors of within-family variants across multiple noise levels under semantic conditioning and attributes the source via normalized calibrated scores. To enable systematic evaluation, we construct DNA-30K, a benchmark for within-family variant attribution under open-set family-level evaluation. It comprises 30,000 images generated by 24 candidate models across six families spanning both denoising diffusion and flow matching, plus non-candidate generated and natural images as unknown sources. Experiments show that DNA achieves 89.11% end-to-end attribution accuracy on a task where random guessing accuracy is below 1% and outperforms the strongest baseline by 33.81% even when AEDR is used as the coarse-grained stage.

</details>


### [62] [Barnamala: Parameter-Efficient Handwritten Devanagari Recognition at Benchmark Saturation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13689)
*Ashish Thapa,Samrat Karki*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A compact CNN (1.11M params) achieves 99.73% on DHCD Devanagari recognition, reaching the intrinsic error floor (11 errors) where no configuration statistically outperforms others. It is 15.6x smaller than prior SOTA, matches large baselines without distillation, and shows superior corruption robustness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve state-of-the-art Devanagari character recognition with a significantly smaller model, investigate whether prior large models have reached a saturation point, and demonstrate that compact architectures can match or exceed large models while offering better efficiency and robustness.

Method: A compact CNN with 1.11M parameters; evaluated against large teacher ensembles and baselines (17.32M params) using exact McNemar tests with Wilson confidence intervals; tested on DHCD (46-class), zero-shot and fine-tuned on CMATERdb digits, and corruption robustness benchmarks.

Result: 99.73% accuracy on DHCD (11-error floor); 15.6x smaller than prior SOTA; no statistically significant difference between any tested configurations; student matches large baseline without distillation (p=0.345); zero-shot 76.6%, fine-tuned 97.8% on CMATERdb; corruption accuracy 75.7% vs. 38.7% for large baselines.

Conclusion: The DHCD dataset has reached a saturation point where model size does not affect performance. Compact CNNs are sufficient for Devanagari recognition, offering superior efficiency, statistical parity with large models, and better robustness to corruptions.

Abstract: We built a compact convolutional network (1.11 M parameters) for 46-class DHCD Devanagari recognition and reached 99.73%, the highest reported at 15.6x smaller than prior state-of-the-art. We have effectively reached the saturation point: every model tested, large teacher ensembles included, hits the same 11-error intrinsic floor. No configuration achieves a statistically clear win under exact McNemar tests with Wilson confidence intervals. Even without knowledge distillation, our student matches the nearest large-model baseline (17.32 M parameters; McNemar $p = 0.345$). Outside of DHCD, zero-shot on CMATERdb digits gives 76.6% and fine-tuning reaches 97.8%; corruption robustness is also far better than large baselines (mean corruption accuracy 75.7% vs. 38.7%). All artifacts are at https://github.com/Ampixa/barnamala.

</details>


### [63] [Anatomically Faithful but Temporally Blind: Auditing Attribution for Left-Ventricular Ejection-Fraction Estimation from Echocardiography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13738)
*Hyunkyung Han,Min Jung Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Deep video models for EF estimation show spatial faithfulness (looking at the left ventricle) but temporal blindness (ignoring clinically decisive ES/ED frames), revealing that post-hoc attribution can certify anatomical grounding while masking critical temporal failures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Post-hoc attribution methods (Chefer relevance, Grad-CAM) are increasingly used to certify that deep video models for EF estimation "look at the right place," but whether these explanations are faithful both spatially and temporally has not been audited. Because EF is defined by ES and ED frames, a faithful explanation must localize both the left ventricle (space) and the decisive frames (time).

Method: Fine-tuned two EF regressors on EchoNet-Dynamic (VideoMAE transformer and R(2+1)D CNN), audited each with architecture-matched attribution along three axes: IoR against LV masks, deletion AUC, and temporal localization index on ES/ED frames, each relative to chance with per-case 95% CIs over 50 studies. Used tubelet-occlusion probe to separate attribution failure from model behavior.

Result: Both models are anatomically faithful (IoR 2.91x for VideoMAE, 1.98x for R(2+1)D above chance) yet temporally blind (temporal localization indistinguishable from chance, 0.97-1.00). Occlusion shows models do not preferentially rely on ES/ED frames (0.90x chance), indicating temporal blindness reflects model behavior, not an attribution artifact.

Conclusion: Spatial faithfulness does not imply temporal faithfulness. Attribution can certify anatomical grounding while masking that a model ignores clinically decisive frames, cautioning against reliance on XAI-based validation for video diagnostic models and calling for temporally-aware training and evaluation.

Abstract: Background and Objective: Deep video models estimate left-ventricular ejection fraction (EF) from echocardiography with near-expert accuracy, and post-hoc attribution (Chefer relevance for transformers, Grad-CAM for CNNs) is increasingly used to certify that models "look at the right place." Yet whether these explanations are faithful both spatially and temporally is unaudited. Because EF is defined by the end-systolic (ES) and end-diastolic (ED) frames, a faithful explanation must localize the left ventricle (space) and the decisive frames (time).
  Methods: We fine-tune two distinct EF regressors on EchoNet-Dynamic -- a self-supervised VideoMAE transformer and a Kinetics-pretrained R(2+1)D CNN -- and audit each with architecture-matched attribution along three axes: intersection-over-relevance (IoR) against LV masks, deletion AUC, and a temporal localization index on ES/ED frames, each relative to chance with per-case 95% CIs over 50 studies. A tubelet-occlusion probe separates attribution failure from model behavior.
  Results: Both models are anatomically faithful -- IoR 2.91x (VideoMAE) and 1.98x (R(2+1)D) above chance -- yet temporally blind: temporal localization is indistinguishable from chance (0.97--1.00) and no better than random attribution. Occlusion shows the models do not preferentially rely on ES/ED (0.90x chance), so temporal blindness reflects model behavior, not an attribution artifact.
  Conclusions: Spatial faithfulness does not imply temporal faithfulness. Attribution can certify anatomical grounding while masking that a model ignores the clinically decisive frames -- a caution for XAI-based validation of video diagnostic models and a call for temporally-aware training and evaluation.

</details>


### [64] [EgoProceVQA: A Novel Egocentric Procedural Understanding Task with Self-Skill-Exploration Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13792)
*Junlong Li,Junxi Li,Yuxiang Yang,Wenbin Zou,Lap-Pui Chau,Yi Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces EgoProceVQA, a benchmark and task for evaluating procedural understanding in egocentric video through key-step-level VQA, along with EgoProceGen (data generation platform) and EgoProceAgent (a self-skill-exploration agentic framework achieving SOTA performance).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing egocentric video evaluations lack procedural understanding and overlook key-step-level reasoning in VQA, which is essential for building procedural AI assistants on wearable devices.

Method: The authors propose: (1) EgoProceVQA task with six types of key-step-centric questions; (2) EgoProceGen data generation platform for constructing QA data; (3) EgoProceAgent, a self-skill-exploration agentic framework with a generic tool library and standardized sub-skill library enabling self-exploration without ground-truth supervision.

Result: Existing MLLMs and agents show substantial room for improvement on EgoProceVQA. The proposed EgoProceAgent achieves state-of-the-art performance among open-source models on multiple tasks.

Conclusion: The benchmark, generation platform, and agentic framework together establish a unified foundation for egocentric procedural understanding VQA.

Abstract: Most daily activities are inherently procedural. However, existing evaluations for egocentric video understanding seldom address procedural understanding and largely overlook complex key-step-level reasoning under the widely used video question answering (VQA) paradigm for MLLMs. Such capabilities are crucial for building procedural AI assistants deployable on wearable devices. To bridge this gap, we introduce the Egocentric Procedural Understanding VQA task (EgoProceVQA), which systematically evaluates egocentric procedural reasoning abilities of current MLLMs and agents through six types of key-step-centric questions. Furthermore, we develop EgoProceGen, a data generation platform that efficiently constructs QA data tailored to different question types. Based on this platform, we build a benchmark with 3,600 questions, four common procedural scenarios, and 31 everyday procedural tasks. Evaluations on EgoProceVQA show that existing MLLMs and agents still have substantial room for improvement in procedural understanding. Therefore, we further propose EgoProceAgent, a self-skill-exploration agentic framework. We design a generic tool library for procedural understanding and a standardized sub-skill library shared across tools and models, enabling self-exploration without ground-truth supervision. By exploring how to compose and select sub-skills, the agent discovers effective skill strategies for diverse problems, and attains state-of-the-art performance among open-source models on multiple tasks. Together, our benchmark, generation platform, and agentic framework establish a unified foundation for EgoProceVQA. Project page: https://z1oong.github.io/EgoProceVQA/.

</details>


### [65] [Prospective clinical indication, post-hoc report leakage, and fusion design in multi-image chest radiograph classification: a patient-clustered evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13800)
*Kamran Shahid,Muhammad Munwar Iqbal*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper evaluates multimodal fusion methods for chest radiograph classification using 15,000 ReXGradient-160K studies, finding that adding clinical Indication text to images improves performance (AUROC 0.780 vs 0.694 for images alone), permutation-aware methods (SectionGuard-MI: AUROC 0.783, DeepSets: 0.787, random-swap: AUPRC 0.265) are competitive, but full report text leads to circularity (AUROC 0.979), indicating leakage from Findings/Impression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether clinical Indication text (produced earlier in care) leaks information from later-stage report text (Findings, Impression) in chest radiograph classification, and to evaluate multimodal fusion methods that respect permutation invariance.

Method: Evaluated 15,000 ReXGradient-160K studies with two images and five CheXbert-derived observations. Compared models: image-only, Indication-only, fixed-order multimodal, random-swap, DeepSets, and SectionGuard-MI, using frozen DenseNet-121 and Bio+ClinicalBERT encoders. Findings/Impression used only as leakage controls. Trained with 5 seeds; uncertainty via 2,000 bootstrap replicates.

Result: Macro AUROC: primary image 0.643, two images 0.694, Indication 0.749, ordinary two-image+Indication fusion 0.780. SectionGuard-MI: AUROC 0.783, AUPRC 0.260. DeepSets highest prospective AUROC (0.787); random-swap highest prospective AUPRC (0.265) with better calibration. Full report: AUROC 0.979, AUPRC 0.836; masked report AUROC >0.973. SectionGuard-MI vs ordinary fusion: AUROC difference 0.0031 (p=0.374), AUPRC difference 0.0289 (p=0.004).

Conclusion: Prospective Indication is strongly associated with report-derived targets; permutation-aware fusion methods (DeepSets, random-swap, SectionGuard-MI) are competitive with ordinary fusion. Post-hoc report text creates substantial report-label circularity, highlighting the need for careful leakage control in multimodal medical AI.

Abstract: Chest radiograph datasets often combine multiple images with Clinical Indication, Findings, and Impression, although these inputs are produced at different stages of care. We evaluated 15,000 ReXGradient-160K studies with two readable images and five CheXbert-derived report observations. Frozen DenseNet-121 and Bio+ClinicalBERT encoders were used to compare image-only, Indication-only, fixed-order multimodal, random-swap, DeepSets, and SectionGuard-MI models. Findings and Impression were evaluated only as post-hoc leakage controls. Models were trained with five seeds, and public-test uncertainty was estimated with 2,000 patient-cluster bootstrap replicates. Under U-Ones, macro AUROC was 0.643 for the primary image, 0.694 for two images, 0.749 for Indication, and 0.780 for ordinary two-image-plus-Indication fusion. SectionGuard-MI achieved AUROC 0.783 and AUPRC 0.260. Relative to ordinary fusion, its paired AUROC difference was 0.0031 (95% CI, -0.0042 to 0.0104; adjusted p=0.374), while its AUPRC difference was 0.0289 (95% CI, 0.0095 to 0.0413; adjusted p=0.004). DeepSets had the highest prospective AUROC point estimate (0.787), and random-swap fusion had the highest prospective AUPRC point estimate (0.265) with better calibration than SectionGuard-MI. Full report text alone reached AUROC 0.979 and AUPRC 0.836; AUROC remained above 0.973 after exact or expanded masking. These results show that prospective Indication is strongly associated with report-derived targets, permutation-aware fusion is competitive, and post-hoc report text creates substantial report-label circularity.

</details>


### [66] [RainDancer: RGB-Event Video Deraining with Rain-Oriented Spiking Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13802)
*Kui Jiang,Runzhe Li,Zhaocheng Yu,Guanglu Sun,Junjun Jiang,Xianming Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RainDancer proposes a decompose-before-interact paradigm for RGB-Event video deraining, separating rain and background in each modality before cross-modal fusion, achieving superior performance on synthetic and real datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: RGB-only video deraining is ambiguous in dynamic rainy scenes because rain streaks, textures, boundaries, motion, and occlusions share similar visual patterns. Event cameras offer motion-sensitive cues but introduce cross-modal interference when fused directly with RGB due to sensor noise and background-triggered responses.

Method: RainDancer is a progressive RGB-Event video deraining framework following a decompose-before-interact paradigm: (1) RGB branch progressively decomposes frame features into rain and background representations; (2) Event branch uses a rain-oriented spiking neural network (SNN) module to capture sparse, bursty event dynamics from rain motion; (3) Component-level fusion aligns semantically matched representations across modalities for structure preservation and rain suppression; (4) Event-domain supervision regularizes sparse event reconstruction, structural consistency, and gradient orientation.

Result: Experiments on synthetic and real RGB-Event video deraining datasets demonstrate superior quantitative performance, visual quality, and downstream perception robustness compared to existing methods.

Conclusion: The decompose-before-interact paradigm effectively mitigates cross-modal interference in RGB-Event fusion for video deraining, and the approach generalizes well to both synthetic and real-world rainy scenes.

Abstract: Video deraining aims to recover clean visual content from rainy videos for reliable perception under adverse weather. Existing methods mainly rely on RGB sequences and temporal redundancy, but RGB-only restoration remains ambiguous in dynamic rainy scenes, where rain streaks, textures, boundaries, motion, and occlusions may share similar visual patterns. Event cameras provide complementary motion-sensitive cues with high temporal resolution, but event streams also contain sensor noise and background-triggered responses, so direct RGB-Event fusion may introduce cross-modal interference. To address this issue, we propose RainDancer, a progressive RGB-Event video deraining framework based on a decompose-before-interact paradigm. The core idea is to separate rain and background components within each modality before cross-modal interaction. In the RGB branch, frame features are progressively decomposed into rain and background representations. In the event branch, a rain-oriented spiking neural network module captures sparse and bursty event dynamics associated with rain motion. Component-level fusion is then performed between semantically aligned representations for structure preservation and rain suppression. We further introduce event-domain supervision to regularize sparse event reconstruction, structural consistency, and gradient orientation. Experiments on synthetic and real RGB-Event video deraining datasets demonstrate superior quantitative performance, visual quality, and downstream perception robustness. Code is available at https://github.com/AE86-plus/RainDancer.

</details>


### [67] [AspectCLIP: Optimizing CLIP Representation Space via Aspect-Guided Consistency Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13805)
*Yiyang Yao,Shanglin Liu,Jianming Lv,Chengjun Wang,Jinyi Li,Yuchan Jie,Zhihua Jin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AspectCLIP addresses information asymmetry in contrastive image-text pretraining by partitioning samples into aspect-coherent clusters based on textual similarity, applying full cyclic consistency within clusters and only prototype-level comparisons across clusters, resulting in improved downstream performance and more structured representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing global consistency regularization methods overlook the information asymmetry between images and text: captions describe only one aspect of an image, so visually similar images can have divergent captions, causing global regularizers to introduce semantic distortion into the representation space.

Method: AspectCLIP partitions training samples into attribute clusters based on textual similarity to identify aspect-coherent groups, then applies full cyclic consistency within each cluster while restricting cross-cluster regularization to prototype-level comparisons.

Result: AspectCLIP consistently outperforms traditional methods on downstream tasks and achieves a more structured representation space.

Conclusion: Respecting the one-to-many structure between images and text through aspect-guided regularization yields better representations than global consistency approaches.

Abstract: Contrastive Language-Image Pretraining learns a shared representation space through large-scale contrastive learning. However, existing methods that enforce global consistency regularization overlook a key challenge: the inherent information asymmetry between images and text: captions typically describe only one specific aspect of an image, thus images with similar visual content can be paired with completely divergent textual content and semantic information. Consequently, global regularizers inadvertently impose constraints between visually similar images whose captions describe divergent aspects, introducing semantic distortion into the representation space. We propose AspectCLIP, a framework that reformulates consistency regularization to respect this one-to-many structure. AspectCLIP first partitions training samples into attribute clusters based on textual similarity to identify aspect-coherent groups, then applies full cyclic consistency within each cluster while restricting cross-cluster regularization to prototype-level comparisons. This aspect-guided regularization enforces strict geometric alignment only when images and texts describe a consistent facet, while allowing flexibility across divergent aspects. Extensive experiments on downstream tasks demonstrate that AspectCLIP consistently outperforms traditional methods and achieves a more structured representation space.

</details>


### [68] [Multimodal Assessment of Pancreatic Cancer Resectability Using Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13826)
*Vincent Ochs,Christoph Kuemmerli,Florentin Bieder,Julia Wolleb,Joel L. Lavanchy,Julia Ruppel,Jan Liechti,Stephanie Taha-Mehlitz,Christian Andreas Nebiker,Beat Mueller,Giuseppe Kito Fusai,Joerg-Matthias Pollok,Anas Taha,Philippe C. Cattin,Sebastian Staubli*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A fully automated multimodal deep learning framework (Swin-UNETR + clinical embedding) that classifies PDAC resectability into three NCCN categories by jointly analyzing 3D CT scans and clinical data, using auxiliary vessel/tumor segmentation and a dynamic multitask objective.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate PDAC resectability assessment depends on CT evaluation of tumor-vessel involvement, but expert assessments exhibit high variability, motivating the need for an automated, standardized approach.

Method: A multimodal framework combining a Swin-UNETR backbone for anatomy-aware image representations (via auxiliary segmentation of pancreas, tumor, and vessels) fused with a compact clinical embedding (17 variables), guided by a dynamic multitask objective that adapts segmentation-classification balance based on tumor Dice performance.

Result: Not explicitly stated in the abstract; the paper presents the framework and its training strategy without reporting quantitative results here.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides anatomy-informed and discriminative feature representations for PDAC resectability classification, potentially reducing variability in expert assessment.

Abstract: Accurate determination of pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma (PDAC) resectability relies on evaluating how the tumor interacts with major peripancreatic vessels on CT imaging, yet expert assessment often shows substantial variability. We introduce a fully automated multimodal deep learning framework that jointly analyzes 3D contrast enhanced CT and structured clinical information to classify patients into the three National Comprehensive Cancer Network (NCCN) resectability categories (upfront resectable, borderline resectable, locally advanced). The approach uses a Swin-UNETR backbone to obtain anatomy aware image representations through auxiliary segmentation of pancreas, tumor, and vascular structures. These features are fused with a compact clinical embedding derived from 17 routinely collected variables and processed by a lightweight classification head. Model training is guided by a dynamic multitask objective that adapts the balance between segmentation and classification based on current tumor Dice performance, promoting feature representations that remain both anatomically informed and discriminative.

</details>


### [69] [Towards Enhancing 3D Spatial Reasoning in Medical Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13860)
*Zhuoyuan Fu,Zeshang Li,Yiqiong Zhang,Hangui Lin,Yan Shu,Yan Li,Binyang Li,Yaru Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces a large-scale structured reasoning dataset for 3D medical imaging using a slice-wise data synthesis paradigm that mimics radiologists' diagnostic workflow. By instruction-tuning a 2D-pretrained MLLM with this Chain-of-Thought data, the model achieves significant performance gains on 3D benchmarks, rivaling native 3D architectures without expensive 3D-specific pre-training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current MLLMs for 3D medical imaging suffer from prohibitive annotation costs, dataset opacity, and reliance on rigid VQA pairs or unstructured clinical reports that fail to capture explicit clinical reasoning.

Method: A slice-wise data synthesis paradigm inspired by radiologists' workflow: decomposes 3D reading into per-slice observations, then synthesizes them into an interpretable Chain-of-Thought (CoT) that enforces sequential spatial tracking, multi-slice spatial awareness for artifact mitigation, and differential exclusion. A standard 2D-pretrained MLLM is instruction-tuned on this synthesized data.

Result: Significant performance improvements over 2D baselines across multiple 3D medical benchmarks; the model exhibits robust spatial reasoning and rivals resource-intensive native 3D architectures, effectively bridging the performance gap.

Conclusion: This data-centric strategy unlocks deep volumetric understanding and interpretable clinical logic without computationally expensive 3D-specific pre-training.

Abstract: While Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have demonstrated remarkable success in 2D medical image understanding, their extension to 3D volumetric imaging remains hindered by prohibitive annotation costs and dataset opacity. Current data formats, predominantly consisting of rigid Visual Question Answering (VQA) pairs or unstructured final clinical reports, typically fail to capture explicit clinical reasoning. To address this limitation, we introduce a large-scale structured reasoning dataset constructed via a novel slice-wise data synthesis paradigm. Inspired by the genuine diagnostic workflow of radiologists, this paradigm models visual cognition by decomposing the complex 3D reading process, translating global clinical priors into fine-grained, per-slice observations that are subsequently synthesized into an interpretable Chain-of-Thought (CoT). Crucially, this synthesized reasoning framework enforces essential clinical principles: sequential spatial tracking, multi-slice spatial awareness for artifact mitigation, and differential exclusion. To validate this approach, we instruction-tune a standard 2D-pretrained MLLM baseline using the synthesized data to enhance its volumetric comprehension. Comprehensive evaluations across multiple 3D medical benchmarks demonstrate that our method yields significant performance improvements over the 2D baseline. Furthermore, the resulting model exhibits robust spatial reasoning capabilities and rivals resource-intensive native 3D architectures, effectively bridging the performance gap. Ultimately, this data-centric strategy unlocks deep volumetric understanding and highly interpretable clinical logic without requiring computationally expensive 3D-specific pre-training. The complete repository, including datasets and training workflows, is publicly available at https://github.com/2020420145009/hounsfield.

</details>


### [70] [Unleashing Multimodal Large Language Models for Training-free HOI Detection in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13881)
*Ting Lei,Jialin Liu,Zhu Xu,Yuxin Peng,Yang Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AgentHOI is a training-free, agentic framework that uses multimodal reasoning of foundation models for open-world human-object interaction detection, introducing Context-aware Multi-round Reasoning and Multifaceted Interaction Localization to outperform supervised/weakly-supervised methods without requiring any HOI detection training data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional HOI detection is limited to closed-set benchmarks and dataset-specific supervision, failing to generalize to open-world and compositional scenarios. Existing MLLM-based prompting strategies focus on discriminative representations but underexploit inherent multimodal reasoning, struggling with ambiguous and open-world interactions.

Method: AgentHOI is a training-free agentic framework that modularly orchestrates complementary vision foundation modules for open-ended semantic reasoning and spatial grounding. It introduces (1) Context-aware Multi-round Reasoning for progressive refinement of interaction hypotheses, and (2) Multifaceted Interaction Localization for instance-specific descriptions integrating semantic, spatial, and appearance cues.

Result: AgentHOI achieves superior performance over state-of-the-art supervised and weakly supervised methods in real-world settings, despite requiring no HOI detection data for training.

Conclusion: By transferring generalist multimodal reasoning from foundation models and using modular coordination of vision components, AgentHOI effectively addresses open-world HOI detection without task-specific training, outperforming methods that rely on supervised learning.

Abstract: Human-object interaction detection (HOID) has traditionally been formulated as a supervised detection problem over predefined interaction categories. While such paradigms achieve strong performance on closed-set benchmarks, they fundamentally entangle interaction understanding with dataset-specific supervision, limiting their ability to generalize to open-world and compositional scenarios. Recent HOI detectors attempt to leverage MLLMs through prompting strategies to transfer interaction-specific knowledge. However, such prompt-based approaches primarily focus on extracting discriminative representations from pretrained models, while underexploring their inherent multimodal reasoning capabilities. As a result, they struggle to provide informative contextual reasoning for ambiguous and open-world interaction scenarios. In this work, we present AgentHOI, a training-free, agentic framework that transfers the generalist multimodal reasoning capabilities of foundation models to HOI detection in the wild. Instead of learning interaction classifiers, AgentHOI modularly orchestrates complementary vision foundation modules to perform open-ended semantic reasoning and spatial grounding in a coordinated manner. To address the challenges of incomplete interaction discovery and ambiguous localization in complex scenes, we introduce two key mechanisms: (1) Context-aware Multi-round Reasoning, which progressively refines interaction hypotheses to ensure exhaustive and compositional HOI discovery, and (2) Multifaceted Interaction Localization, which enhances grounding precision by generating instance-specific descriptions that integrate semantic, spatial, and appearance cues. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AgentHOI achieves superior performance over state-of-the-art supervised and weakly supervised methods in real-world settings, despite requiring no HOID data for training.

</details>


### [71] [Fine-Grained Vision-Language Pretraining with Organ-Conditioned Pattern Tokens for CT Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13892)
*Guoliang You,Xiaomeng Chu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OCP-CT introduces an organ-conditioned pattern-token alignment framework for CT vision-language pretraining, using sparse Mixture-of-Experts and learnable slots to align image-text tokens at the radiological pattern level, achieving state-of-the-art zero-shot abnormality diagnosis on CT-RATE and RAD-ChestCT benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing CT vision-language pretraining methods use global scan-report contrast (scalable but obscures heterogeneous organ evidence) or direct organ-level alignment (coarse, since same anatomy can exhibit multiple distinct radiological appearances). A finer alignment unit—the organ-conditioned radiological pattern—is needed.

Method: OCP-CT preserves a global CT-report contrastive branch and introduces an organ pattern interface: (1) sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) routes image and text tokens according to latent radiological patterns, (2) learnable slots query routed tokens into continuous pattern tokens, and (3) paired token contrast aligns image-text pattern tokens with structured soft targets built from report-derived clinical similarity.

Result: On CT-RATE and RAD-ChestCT benchmarks, OCP-CT achieves average AUROCs of 84.5% and 69.9% for zero-shot abnormality diagnosis, yielding absolute AUROC gains of 6.7 and 0.8 percentage points over the strongest prior reported results.

Conclusion: Organ-conditioned pattern-token alignment at the radiological pattern level outperforms prior global and organ-level approaches, demonstrating the benefit of finer-grained alignment units in CT vision-language pretraining.

Abstract: Computed tomography (CT) vision-language pretraining from paired volumes and radiology reports is a scalable yet challenging task. Existing methods commonly adopt global scan-report contrast, which is scalable but obscures heterogeneous organ evidence. Meanwhile, direct organ-level alignment remains coarse, since the same anatomy can exhibit multiple distinct radiological appearances. Therefore, pretraining requires a finer alignment unit: the organ-conditioned radiological pattern. In this work, we propose OCP-CT, an organ-conditioned pattern-token alignment framework for CT vision-language pretraining. Specifically, OCP-CT preserves a stable global CT-report contrastive branch and introduces an organ pattern interface: sparse Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) routes image and text tokens according to latent radiological patterns, learnable slots query the routed tokens into continuous pattern tokens, and paired token contrast aligns image-text pattern tokens with structured soft targets built from report-derived clinical similarity. On the publicly available CT-RATE and RAD-ChestCT benchmarks, OCP-CT achieves average AUROCs of 84.5% and 69.9% for zero-shot abnormality diagnosis, respectively. Compared with the strongest prior reported results, these results yield absolute AUROC gains of 6.7 and 0.8 percentage points.

</details>


### [72] [The 2nd International StepUP Competition for Biometric Footstep Recognition: From Steps to Strides](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13905)
*Robyn Larracy,Anant Gupta,Gourav Gupta,Ethan Eddy,Maxime Devanne,Cyril Meyer,Jin-Chern Chiou,Yueh-Shan Lee,Zong-Han Lu,Aaron Tabor,Erik Scheme*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The 2nd International StepUP Competition advanced pressure-based footstep biometrics using the StepUP-P150 dataset (200K+ footsteps, 150 subjects), addressing generalization, domain shift (footwear/speed), and left-right foot fusion. Top solution (ArogyaPandit) achieved 8.00% EER using spatiotemporal CNN with ensemble scoring, but recognizing unseen personal footwear remains challenging.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance pressure-based footstep biometrics research by creating a standardized, challenging evaluation framework that addresses real-world challenges like unseen users, domain shifts (footwear/speed variations), and footstep pair fusion.

Method: Using the StepUP-P150 dataset (200K+ high-resolution dynamic footsteps from 150 individuals) with a previously unreleased test set; introduced more extreme cross-domain conditions and stride-level verification (vs isolated footsteps in the first edition).

Result: 26 registrants participated; best equal error rate (EER) of 8.00% by ArogyaPandit Research Team using spatiotemporal CNN with ensemble-based scoring strategy.

Conclusion: Top solutions demonstrate the value of temporal pattern exploitation and inference-time normalization/calibration. However, recognizing users in unseen personal footwear remains challenging, especially with similar distractors.

Abstract: The International StepUP Competition Series was launched to advance research in pressure-based footstep biometrics through a standardized and challenging evaluation framework. Using the large-scale StepUP-P150 dataset (with more than 200,000 high-resolution dynamic footsteps from 150 individuals) and a previously unreleased test set, the 2nd edition of the competition addressed three key challenges: (1) generalization to unseen users with limited enrollment data, (2) robustness to domain shift caused by variations in footwear and walking speed and (3) effective fusion of paired left-right footsteps. While the first two challenges built on the inaugural competition, this edition introduced more extreme cross-domain conditions and moved beyond isolated footsteps to stride-level verification, enabling new opportunities for representation learning and inter-step information fusion. The competition attracted 26 registrants from academia and industry, with a best equal error rate of 8.00% achieved by the ArogyaPandit Research Team using a spatiotemporal CNN combined with an ensemble-based scoring strategy. The top solutions showcase the value of harnessing temporal patterns and of incorporating inference-time normalization and calibration strategies to improve scoring. However, the results also reveal that recognizing users in unseen personal footwear remains a challenge, especially in the presence of distractors with similar characteristics.

</details>


### [73] [Thresholded Cross-Attention for Reliable Intensity-Chromaticity Fusion in Low-Light Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13925)
*Yanyi Wu,Xu Zhang,Junkai Chen,Laibin Chang,Jiaqi Ma,Shi Chen,Linwei Zhu,Jianglei Di,Huan Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TCA-Net introduces Thresholded Cross-Attention for reliable intensity-chromaticity fusion in HVI space, replacing fixed Top-K sparse attention with an adaptive confidence threshold, achieving competitive LLIE performance with improved color fidelity and compact parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing HVI-based LLIE methods decouple intensity and chromaticity but overlook the unreliable fusion of the two streams; fixed-quota Top-K sparse attention mismatches layer-dependent confidence, discarding informative dependencies in some layers while retaining noisy ones in others.

Method: TCA-Net proposes: (1) Thresholded Cross-Attention (TCA) replacing fixed Top-K quota with a layer- and input-adaptive confidence threshold; (2) Phase-guided Fourier Interaction Module for structure-aware brightness initialization; (3) Decoupled Dual-Stream Guidance Module for residual intensity features to suppress chromaticity leakage; (4) Scale-Aware Consistency Regularization for structural robustness.

Result: Extensive experiments on LOL-v1, LOL-v2, Sony-Total-Dark, and LSRW-Huawei demonstrate competitive restoration accuracy, improved color fidelity, and compact parameter size.

Conclusion: TCA-Net effectively addresses unreliable intensity-chromaticity fusion in HVI space via adaptive thresholded cross-attention, delivering strong LLIE performance with efficient parameter usage.

Abstract: Low-Light Image Enhancement (LLIE) requires a careful balance among noise suppression, color fidelity, and efficiency. Recent HVI-based methods alleviate color entanglement by decoupling intensity and chromaticity, yet how reliably the two streams are fused again is an overlooked factor that largely determines the final quality. We observe that the confidence of cross-stream attention is strongly layer-dependent, so the fixed-quota selection of Top-K sparse attention is mismatched to it, discarding informative dependencies in some layers while retaining noisy ones in others. Motivated by this observation, we propose TCA-Net, a network built around Thresholded Cross-Attention that targets reliable intensity-chromaticity fusion in the HVI space rather than introducing yet another color representation. At its core, TCA replaces the rigid Top-K quota with a fixed confidence threshold whose retained cardinality is input- and layer-adaptive, retaining only high-confidence cross-stream interactions while suppressing unreliable ones. Around this core, two complementary designs clean up the fusion before and after it: a Phase-guided Fourier Interaction Module provides a structure-aware brightness initialization for the intensity stream prior to fusion, and a Decoupled Dual-Stream Guidance Module constructs residual intensity features to suppress chromaticity leakage during reconstruction. A Scale-Aware Consistency Regularization further improves structural robustness under scale perturbations during training. Extensive experiments on LOL-v1, LOL-v2, Sony-Total-Dark, and LSRW-Huawei demonstrate that TCA-Net delivers competitive restoration accuracy, improved color fidelity, and a compact parameter size.

</details>


### [74] [Cyclone: Diffusion Model for Cycle-Consistent Weather Editing from Unpaired Driving Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13927)
*Thang-Anh-Quan Nguyen,Moussab Bennehar,Luis Guillermo Roldao Jimenez,Nathan Piasco,Dzmitry Tsishkou,Laurent Caraffa,Jean-Philippe Tarel,Roland Brémond*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Cyclone is a unified latent diffusion framework with cycle-consistent constraints and image-text model knowledge that enables realistic, structure-preserving weather editing for autonomous driving perception, outperforming existing baselines and improving downstream task performance without requiring paired training data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reliable autonomous driving perception under diverse weather conditions remains challenging; existing methods rely on synthetic data augmentation or physics-based task-specific models that require paired data, struggle with realistic generation, and generalize poorly to out-of-domain scenarios.

Method: Cyclone is a unified framework based on latent diffusion, incorporating cycle-consistent constraints and leveraging knowledge from image-text models to enable multi-weather condition editing across diverse scenes without paired training data.

Result: Cyclone produces more realistic, structure-preserving weather-edited outputs than existing baselines and yields consistent improvements across several downstream driving perception tasks; it can also be distilled to a video diffusion model for temporally consistent weather editing.

Conclusion: Cyclone effectively addresses the limitations of prior weather-robustness approaches by providing unpaired, realistic, and generalizable weather editing that benefits downstream autonomous driving perception.

Abstract: Reliable perception under diverse weather conditions remains a major challenge for autonomous driving systems. A common strategy to improve robustness is either to synthesize adverse weather conditions for training perception models or to apply weather-removal techniques to recover clean inputs. However, existing approaches typically rely on synthetic data augmentation or physics-based, task-specific models that require paired training data and often struggle to generate realistic weather effects or generalize robustly to out-of-domain scenarios. Toward this problem, we present Cyclone, a unified framework for weather editing based on latent diffusion, equipped with cycle-consistent constraints and knowledge from image-text models. Cyclone enables the generation of multiple weather conditions across diverse scenes while eliminating the need for paired data. Experimental results show that our approach produces more realistic, structure-preserving outputs than existing baselines and leads to consistent improvements across several downstream driving perception tasks. Furthermore, we demonstrate that Cyclone can be distilled to a video diffusion model for temporally consistent weather editing.

</details>
